# canaryfield contract

This contract is the executable spec for canaryfield. Every obligation below maps to at least one named test in the test suite. An obligation is met only when its tests pass.

## Setup obligations

- SETUP-1 The seed files stay intact. README.md keeps the seed description and licence sections, LICENSE keeps the MIT text, and .gitignore keeps the seed rules. Proven by test_seed_files_present.
- SETUP-2 The README status section tells the truth about the build state. It states the local seed, the blocked remote seed, the contract state, the slice state, the closed round count, and the pending AGENTS.md. Proven by test_readme_status_is_honest.
- SETUP-3 Continuous integration runs the same command as the local gate. The workflow runs python -m pytest on push and on pull requests. Proven by test_ci_runs_the_local_gate.
- SETUP-4 No AGENTS.md exists in this repo until the canonical template is supplied by the main chat. Proven by test_no_agents_md_yet. Amended by the setup completion record of 2026-11-01 below, which states the condition met and renames the proving test.
- SETUP-5 Every fixture and example in this repo is synthetic and written for this repo. No real traces, real names, or private detail appear anywhere. Proven by the sanitization self scan reported with every change.

Setup completion, 2026-11-01. The canonical template SETUP-4 waited on was authored once for the whole program by the Gate Truth Line owner chat, under David's direction on 2026-11-01 after the main chat never supplied one, and lives at the canonical path in the program's files. AGENTS.md now stands in this repo, the template copied verbatim above its marked line with this repo's annex below it, and the proving test is renamed test_agents_md_stands_from_the_canonical_template, asserting the file stands with its marked line and annex in place. SETUP-4's condition is met and the obligation is read as satisfied from this record onward.

## Slice obligations

- SLICE-1 The seed registry holds exactly twelve seeds with unique stable ids. Seed zero has id seed-00 and plants the stale version string 9.9.9. Proven by test_registry_has_twelve_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string.
- SLICE-2 Every seed carries a name, a defect class, and an applicability check. A seed that does not apply to a target is recorded as not applicable and is never counted as caught or waved through. Proven by test_seed_metadata_is_complete and test_inapplicable_seed_is_recorded_not_applicable.
- SLICE-3 Planting happens only in scratch copies. The target tree is byte identical after any run, proven by a hash walk taken before and after. Proven by test_target_tree_is_byte_identical_after_a_run.
- SLICE-4 Gates are named commands supplied by the operator. Every gate runs once against a clean scratch copy and once against each planted scratch copy, each run under a timeout. Proven by test_gate_runs_on_clean_and_planted_copies.
- SLICE-5 Catch scoring follows one rule. A seed is caught for a gate when the gate exits zero on the clean copy and non-zero on the planted copy. A seed is waved through when the gate exits zero on both copies. Proven by test_caught_and_waved_scoring.
- SLICE-6 A gate that fails on the clean copy is reported as gate broken for every seed. A broken gate is never reported as catching anything. Proven by test_gate_failing_on_clean_copy_is_broken.
- SLICE-7 The catch table is the report. It carries one row per seed and one column per gate, with each cell holding exactly one of caught, waved through, not applicable, or gate broken. The same facts render as plain text and as JSON. Proven by test_catch_table_shape_and_statuses and test_report_renders_text_and_json_with_the_same_facts.
- SLICE-8 Slice 1 proves the machinery on synthetic targets only. Nothing is planted in a live tree, and the scratch clone proving run against a real portfolio tree is future work, stated as such in the README. Proven by test_example_target_is_synthetic and by the README honesty test in SETUP-2.

## Slice 2 obligations

- SLICE2-1 A git branch planting mode exists beside the copy mode. In git mode the tool clones the target into a scratch clone under the work root and plants only inside that clone. The target itself is never checked out, committed to, or written to. Proven by test_git_mode_target_git_state_untouched.
- SLICE2-2 In git mode every applicable seed is committed on its own throwaway branch in the scratch clone. The branch name is canaryfield/seed- plus the seed id. Gates run in a checkout of that branch. A seed that does not apply gets no branch and stays recorded as not applicable. Proven by test_git_mode_plants_each_seed_on_its_own_branch and test_git_mode_inapplicable_seed_gets_no_branch.
- SLICE2-3 Git mode scoring follows the same rule as copy mode. A seed is caught when the gate exits zero on the clean checkout and non-zero on the seed branch checkout, and waved through when it exits zero on both. A gate that fails on the clean checkout is gate broken and catches nothing. Proven by test_git_mode_scores_caught_and_waved.
- SLICE2-4 The scratch clone is deleted at the end of a run unless the operator asks to keep it. Proven by test_git_mode_scratch_clone_deleted_by_default and test_git_mode_scratch_clone_kept_on_request.
- SLICE2-5 Copy mode does not change. The slice 1 example catch table regenerates byte identical under copy mode, and every slice 1 obligation above keeps passing. Proven by test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical.
- SLICE2-6 Two gates can judge the same field in one run and their verdicts are reported side by side. A second committed example shows two gates over the same target with at least one seed where the gates honestly disagree. Proven by test_two_gate_example_shows_an_honest_disagreement and test_two_gate_run_scores_each_gate_on_its_own_evidence.
- SLICE2-7 The seed registry can be written out as a versioned seed pack file. The pack carries the format string canaryfield-seeds/1 and one entry per seed with id, name, defect class, and description. Loading the pack returns the same twelve ids and defect classes as the built in registry. Proven by test_seed_pack_round_trip and test_seed_pack_via_cli.
- SLICE2-8 Git mode refuses a target that is not a git repository. The refusal is a clear error before any planting happens, never a silent fall back to copy mode. Proven by test_git_mode_requires_a_git_target.

## Round 1 review fix obligations

The Round 1 audit and red team found ways a run could harm material outside its own scratch and ways its refusals and pack loading were weaker than the claims around them. The obligations below amend the slice obligations where they conflict. Each one is proven by tests in tests/test_round1_fixes.py unless it says otherwise.

- FIX2-1 A run removes only what it created. A supplied work root must start empty and a run refuses a non empty work root before doing any work, on success and on failure paths alike, so a failed run can never delete a previously kept clone or an unrelated operator file. A work root the run created itself is removed whole, and a pre existing empty work root keeps only what was already inside it, which is nothing the run did not place there.
- FIX2-2 Git mode isolates its seeds from its gates. The scratch clone checkout is hard reset and cleaned between the clean run and every seed, so a gate that writes tracked or untracked files inside the checkout cannot abort the run or poison a later seed. Gates are still expected to be read only judges, and the engine no longer depends on that expectation.
- FIX2-3 The seed pack loader enforces the registry invariants it mirrors. A pack of this format carries exactly twelve entries, every entry field is a non empty piece of text, and ids are unique. A pack that breaks any of those rules is refused.
- FIX2-4 Git mode refusals name the actual problem. A target inside another repository but not at its root is refused as not the root, a repository with no commits is refused as having no commits, and the command line surface prints refusals as one error line with a non zero exit and never as a traceback.
- FIX2-5 The two planting modes judge different material and the contract says so. Git mode judges the committed HEAD of the target because a clone carries only committed state, and copy mode judges the working tree as it stands. An operator with uncommitted edits gets different content scored by the two modes, by construction.

## Slice 3 obligations

Slice 3 changes how widely a seed plants. Earlier slices planted a seed into the first file it applied to and stopped, so a defect class that lived in five files was only ever planted in one of them and the catch table said nothing about the other four. The obligations below state the wider behaviour and amend the earlier planting text where the two conflict.

- G-1 A seed plants into every file where it applies. In both planting modes the seed plants into every non test Python file in the scratch material where its applicability check succeeds, one planting per file at the first matching shape in that file, exactly as a planting inside one file worked before. This obligation amends the planting behaviour behind SLICE-3 and SLICE-4 and the planting step in SLICE2-2, which planted only into the first applicable file, and the amendment is stated here rather than left as a silent conflict between the two texts. Proven by test_plant_seed_covers_every_applicable_file and test_multi_file_fixture_names_every_planted_file.
- G-2 Every result row names every planted file. Each row carries planted_files holding the repo relative paths the seed planted into, in sorted order, and the list is empty when the seed applied nowhere and the row is not applicable. The row keeps planted_file as the first entry of that list, or nothing when the list is empty, so readers of the earlier report shape keep working. The text report keeps the same row and status vocabulary and adds the full planted list under a row whenever a seed planted into more than one file, and the JSON report carries the same list. Proven by test_multi_file_fixture_names_every_planted_file and test_report_lists_every_planted_file_in_text_and_json.
- G-3 Scoring is unchanged under plant everywhere. A gate still judges the fully planted copy or branch as one state, caught when the clean run exits zero and the planted run exits non zero, waved through when both exit zero, gate broken when the clean run fails, exactly as SLICE-5 and SLICE2-3 state. Proven by test_multi_file_fixture_scores_the_fully_planted_state and by the unchanged slice 1 and slice 2 scoring tests.
- G-4 A seed that applies to exactly one file behaves exactly as it did in slice 2. Its planted_files holds that one file, its planted_file equals it, and every verdict on a single file fixture matches the verdict slice 2 produced on the same fixture. Proven by test_single_file_fixture_matches_slice_two_verdicts.
- G-5 In git branch mode the seed commit carries every planted file. The one commit on each throwaway branch holds every file that seed planted, the branch naming from SLICE2-2 is unchanged, and the target git state and files stay byte identical under the work root rules from FIX2-1 and the checkout isolation from FIX2-2. Proven by test_git_mode_branch_commit_carries_every_planted_file and test_git_mode_target_git_state_untouched.
- G-6 The committed examples tell the truth under plant everywhere. The slice 1 example catch table, the two gate example tables, and the seed pack are regenerated through the command line after the planting change, and the README status records any example verdict that changed because of the wider planting and the reason it changed. This obligation amends the claim in SLICE2-5 that copy mode does not change, because copy mode planting is exactly what widened. Proven by test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical and test_two_gate_run_scores_each_gate_on_its_own_evidence.

## Round 2 review fixes

The Round 2 red team rejected the slice as first built, because the wider walk reopened a harm Round 1 had closed. A run could write planted content outside the scratch material through a symlink, silently, and three further claims broke on shapes the ordinary fixtures never held. The two axis review added one ordering defect of its own. The obligations below amend the slice 3 obligations and the earlier ones they name where they conflict, and each one is covered by tests in tests/test_round2_fixes.py unless it says otherwise.

Obligation FIX3-1 covers symlink safety. A candidate file that is a symlink is never planted into, and a candidate whose resolution lands outside the scratch root is never planted into, in either mode. A run therefore cannot write through a link to material outside the scratch copies or the scratch clone, and a relative symlink to another file inside the tree is a spelling of that file and never a second planting counted under its own name.

Obligation FIX3-2 covers what counts as a planting. A file counts as planted only when the seed changes its syntax tree. A shape that is already in its planted form, the way a version literal already reading 9.9.9 meets the stale version seed, plants nothing, and when no file changes the seed is not applicable and no branch is made in git mode. The seed commit in git mode therefore always carries changes, and it carries exactly the files the row names.

Obligation FIX3-3 covers test infrastructure. The test file test now also excludes a file named conftest.py, a file named exactly test.py, and any file under a directory named test or testing, beside the shapes it already excluded. A defect can no longer be planted into the harness that runs the gate and scored as a catch against the field.

Obligation FIX3-4 covers unparseable files. A Python file that cannot be parsed is skipped for every seed instead of ending the run, and the report names every such file in an unparseable files list in the JSON report and on a line of its own in the text report when the list is not empty. One stray unparseable file now costs that file its plantings and nothing else. The example JSON reports gain the same list, empty for the demo project, and no verdict in them changes.

Obligation FIX3-5 covers the planted list order. The planted files list is sorted as the rendered repo relative path strings, which is the order G-2 states, and the case where the two orders part, a module file beside a directory that shares its stem, is pinned by a test.

Obligation FIX3-6 covers seed pack field text. Every field of a seed pack entry must hold text with at least one non whitespace character, so a description of spaces alone is refused the way an empty string already was.

## Slice 4 obligations

Slice 4 changes how widely a seed plants inside one file. Slice 3 planted a seed into every file where it applies and stopped at the first matching shape inside each file, so a file where the shape occurs five times received one defect and the gate never faced the other four. The obligations below state the wider behaviour and amend G-1 where the two conflict.

Obligation H-1 covers planting at every matching shape. When a seed plants into a file it plants at every place in that file where its shape matches, not only at the first. This obligation amends G-1 where the two conflict, because G-1 states one planting per file at the first matching shape in that file, and the amendment is stated here rather than left as a silent conflict between the two texts. The change each seed makes at one match is the change it has always made, and only the number of matches planted in one pass changes. A file still counts as planted exactly when the seed changes its syntax tree, as FIX3-2 states, so a file where every occurrence already sits in its planted form plants nothing, while a file where only some occurrences sit in planted form still plants the rest. The planted list still names each planted file once in the order G-2 and FIX3-5 state. Proven by test_three_occurrences_in_one_file_are_all_planted, test_a_partly_planted_file_plants_the_rest, and test_git_mode_branch_commit_carries_the_fully_planted_file.

Obligation H-2 covers where the matching rule lives. Each seed keeps its matching rule and its change in its own apply function in canaryfield/seeds.py, and one call to that function plants at every match in the tree it is given and reports whether it planted anything. plant_seed in canaryfield/engine.py calls the apply function once per file and never repeats a call to reach further matches, so what matches and how it changes is defined in exactly one place for each seed. Proven by test_one_apply_call_plants_every_match.

Obligation H-3 covers the one coherent pass. The matches for one file are collected from the tree as parsed, before any change is made, and each matched node is planted exactly once in that pass. A change made by a planting is never rescanned during the same pass, so a planting cannot create a new match for itself, and a planted shape that would itself match is left exactly as planted and never planted a second time. A matched node that an earlier planting in the same pass removes from the tree is changed in memory and is no longer part of the written file, so the written file shows the outer planting. Proven by test_a_planted_shape_is_not_planted_twice, test_negated_conditions_wrap_exactly_once, test_range_bounds_shift_exactly_once, and test_a_match_inside_a_swallowed_handler_stays_detached.

Obligation H-4 covers the proof that the widening is real. A fixture whose gate exercises only the second occurrence of a shape is waved through when only the first shape is planted and is caught when every shape is planted, and a fixture with three occurrences of one shape in one file shows all three planted in the planted content and is judged on that fully planted file. Proven by test_second_occurrence_only_fixture_flips_with_the_widening and test_three_occurrences_in_one_file_are_all_planted.

Obligation H-5 covers stability everywhere else. A seed that matches at exactly one shape in a file plants exactly as it did in slice 3, the single occurrence fixtures keep their slice 3 verdicts, and the scoring rule from G-3 and the report shape from G-2 do not change. The committed examples regenerate byte identical under the wider planting unless a verdict honestly changes, and any such change is recorded with its reason in the README status in the same change. Proven by test_single_occurrence_fixture_keeps_its_slice_three_verdicts and by the unchanged slice 1, slice 2, and slice 3 tests, the round 1 and round 2 fix tests, and the example regeneration tests.

## Round 3 review fixes

The Round 3 red team found no defect in the widening itself and two defects the wider planting inherited and multiplied, and the standards axis of the two axis review found one disclosed behaviour change carrying no pinning test. The obligations below amend FIX3-1, H-1 and H-2 where they conflict, and each one is covered by tests in tests/test_round3_fixes.py unless it says otherwise.

Obligation FIX4-1 covers symlinks in copy mode. The tree copy preserves symlinks instead of dereferencing them, so a link reaches the planting walk as a link and the guard of FIX3-1 fires in copy mode exactly as it fires in git mode. A relative link to another file inside the tree is therefore never a second planting counted under its own name, and a link pointing at a file outside the target is never planted or judged as field content. Before this fix the copy dereferenced every link, so the guard never saw one in copy mode and an alias was planted and counted under the link name.

Obligation FIX4-2 covers the scope of the wrong variable seed. A return is judged against the parameter list of the function whose own body holds it. The walk that collects a function's returns stops at any nested function or class, and a nested function is judged against its own parameters when the walk of the tree reaches it. Before this fix the walk descended into nested bodies, so a return inside a one parameter nested function was rewritten against the outer function's parameters into a name unbound in its own scope, and the gate catch that followed was a name error rather than the documented defect.

Obligation FIX4-3 covers the swallowed exception seed and two readings of the slice 4 text. A handler already reduced to a bare pass is skipped by that seed and the later handlers in the file are planted, and that behaviour is pinned by a named test rather than resting on disclosure alone. Obligation H-1 is read with the carve out H-3 states, so a match an earlier planting in the same pass removes from the tree is changed in memory and never reaches the written file, and the written file shows the outer planting alone. The every match rule lives in each seed apply function rather than in a repeating loop in plant_seed for the reason H-2 gives and one more, which is that several seeds match their own planting, so a loop that repeated a call until nothing matched would never terminate.

## Slice 5 obligations

Slice 5 grows the seed registry. The twelve seeds of the earlier slices cover many defect families and leave real ones unplanted, and the seed pack format has no room to grow because FIX2-3 pins its single version to exactly twelve entries. The obligations below add four seeds and version the pack so both counts stay pinned, each to its own format. The obligations amend SLICE-1 and SLICE2-7 and FIX2-3 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts. Two candidate families were set aside while the four were chosen, and the reasons belong in this record. An inverted membership test was set aside because seed-02 already flips in to not in inside its comparison table, so a membership seed would have planted a family the registry already holds. A mutated mutable default argument was set aside because the defect lives in how a body uses a shared default across calls and has no single node shape to collect, so planting it would have meant rewriting function bodies by pattern rather than planting one collected match per occurrence.

Obligation I-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds sixteen seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-15. The original twelve seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends SLICE-1, which pinned the registry at exactly twelve. Proven by test_registry_has_sixteen_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation I-2 covers seed-12, swapped operand order. The seed plants at every binary operation whose operator is subtraction or division or floor division or modulo by exchanging the left and right operands. Those operators are not commutative, so the order of the operands decides the result and an exchanged pair computes a different value from the same inputs while the expression still reads as plausible code. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed and each is planted exactly once in one pass, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3, so an operation nested inside another matched operation is planted once at each level and never twice at the same level. Proven by test_seed_twelve_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twelve_plants_at_two_occurrences_in_one_file and test_seed_twelve_not_applicable_without_a_noncommutative_operator.

Obligation I-3 covers seed-13, dropped else branch. The seed plants at every if statement that carries a non empty else branch by removing the else branch. The else branch carries the fallback path for the inputs the condition rejects, and removing it makes those inputs fall through to whatever follows the statement, silently taking a path the code never wrote for them. An elif chain lives in the else branch as one nested if, so removing the else branch removes the whole chain, and the seed says so by its shape rather than sparing chains. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as I-2. Proven by test_seed_thirteen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_thirteen_not_applicable_without_an_else_branch.

Obligation I-4 covers seed-14, swapped boolean operator. The seed plants at every boolean operation by exchanging and for or and or for and. A compound condition with the wrong connective admits and rejects the wrong cases while every comparison inside it stays right, so the defect hides behind correct looking parts. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as I-2. Proven by test_seed_fourteen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_fourteen_not_applicable_without_a_compound_condition.

Obligation I-5 covers seed-15, dropped await. The seed plants at every await expression by replacing the await with the expression it awaited. Without the await the caller holds a coroutine object instead of the result it waited for, and the awaited work is handed to no scheduler by that expression, so the value is wrong in kind and the work may never run. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed with their parents, and each is planted exactly once, deepest first, so an await nested inside another await is planted at each level without the outer planting stranding the inner one on a detached node. Proven by test_seed_fifteen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_fifteen_not_applicable_without_an_await.

Obligation I-6 covers the versioned pack. Writing a seed pack now writes the format canaryfield-seeds/2 carrying sixteen entries, one per seed in the grown registry. The loader accepts both formats and holds each to its own count, refusing a canaryfield-seeds/1 pack that does not carry exactly twelve entries and a canaryfield-seeds/2 pack that does not carry exactly sixteen, and refusing any other format string the way it always has. The field rules of FIX2-3 and FIX3-6 apply to both versions unchanged, so every entry of either version carries an id and a name and a defect class and a description, each a non empty piece of text, and ids are unique within the pack. This obligation amends SLICE2-7 and FIX2-3, which spoke of one format carrying exactly twelve entries. Proven by test_seed_pack_round_trip and test_seed_pack_via_cli and test_version_one_pack_still_loads_the_original_twelve and test_a_pack_whose_count_disagrees_with_its_version_is_refused.

Obligation I-7 covers the committed examples under the grown registry. The slice 1 catch table and the two gate tables and the seed pack are regenerated through the command line after the registry change, and each table now carries sixteen rows and the pack is a version 2 pack. No verdict on the original twelve seeds changes on the demo project, because each seed still plants alone in its own copy or branch and a new seed cannot reach into another seed planting. The README status records what the new seeds honestly score on the demo project. This obligation amends the example stability readings of SLICE2-5 and G-6 and H-5 for this slice the way those obligations amended them before. Proven by test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical and test_two_gate_example_shows_an_honest_disagreement and test_two_gate_run_scores_each_gate_on_its_own_evidence.

## Round 4 review fixes

The Round 4 review recorded two Minor findings and one design question for the owner, and the obligations below close them. The red team approved the slice with no Critical and no Major findings.

Obligation FIX5-1 covers the pack loader and the ids it accepts, and it records an owner decision. A pack version is a count and field contract. The loader holds each version to its entry count and applies the field rules of FIX2-3 and FIX3-6 to every entry, and it does not compare the ids a hand written pack carries against the registry for that version, so a version 1 pack carrying ids from the grown registry loads as data, and the round trip tests pin registry equality only for packs this tool writes. The sentence in SLICE2-7 about loading returning the same twelve ids and defect classes describes that round trip over the built in registry rather than a promise about hand written packs, and this obligation records that reading so the two texts cannot be taken to promise more than the loader does. Pinning each version to its id set is recorded as debt for a later round rather than decided here, because refusing hand written packs on id grounds would change what the format accepts beyond what any obligation in this round claims.

The slice 5 preamble now records why two candidate families were set aside, an inverted membership test because seed-02 already flips in to not in, and a mutated mutable default argument because the defect has no single node shape to collect. The README status lines from the slice 2 era are reworded in this pass so the two gate example reads at its current eleven disagreements and the seed pack bullet names both format versions. Seed-14 gains a content level test in tests/test_round4_fixes.py that pins its swapped operators in the rendered tree, bringing it to the same standard as its three sibling seeds.

Amendment note on FIX5-1. Slice 6 supersedes the decision recorded in FIX5-1 on the one point it left open. The loader now pins each version to its id set and to the definition fields of its entries under obligations J-1 and J-2, so the reading recorded there, that a version 1 pack carrying ids from the grown registry loads as data, no longer holds. The rest of FIX5-1 stands, including its reading of the SLICE2-7 sentence as a statement about the round trip this tool writes.

## Slice 6 obligations

Slice 6 pays the debt FIX5-1 recorded. That obligation held a pack version to its entry count and its field rules and expressly left the ids a hand written pack carries unverified against the registry for that version, recording the pinning of each version to its id set as debt for a later round. This round pays it, and slice 6 also settles how far the verification reaches into the other entry fields. The obligations below amend FIX5-1 and I-6 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation J-1 covers the id set of a pack. The loader refuses a pack whose ids are not exactly the id set of its declared format version, the original twelve ids seed-00 to seed-11 for canaryfield-seeds/1 and the sixteen ids seed-00 to seed-15 for canaryfield-seeds/2. The order of the entries in the file does not matter for acceptance, because the comparison is a set comparison. A missing id, an invented id, and a grown registry id carried inside a version 1 pack each refuse the pack. The set rule composes with the rules that already stand rather than replacing any of them, so the format check and the count check of I-6 run first, the field rules of FIX2-3 and FIX3-6 and the duplicate id refusal run next over every entry, and the set comparison runs last of those over a pack that has passed all of them, with the definition comparison of J-2 after it, and no refusal path an earlier obligation promised is lost or reworded. The refusal is a named error in the style of the count refusal and says the pack ids do not match its format. Proven by test_a_version_one_pack_carrying_a_grown_registry_id_is_refused and test_a_version_one_pack_with_an_invented_id_is_refused and test_a_version_two_pack_with_an_invented_id_is_refused and test_pack_entry_order_does_not_matter_for_acceptance.

Obligation J-2 covers the definition fields of an entry. For every entry the loader also compares the name and the defect class against the registry seed that carries the same id within the pack version, and refuses the pack when either one disagrees, naming the entry and the field in the error. Those two fields are the definition a consumer reads, because the pack exists so other tools in the line can consume the same seed definitions, and a pack whose seed-03 carries a different defect class than the registry seed-03 is a different registry wearing the right ids, scored under labels its seeds never had. The description is not compared, because it is prose written for a human reader and two wordings of one description do not make two registries, and pinning prose would refuse packs over wording edits that change no definition. The unverified description is a stated limit of the format and is pinned as one, so the limit cannot tighten silently in a later round. Proven by test_a_pack_entry_whose_defect_class_disagrees_with_the_registry_is_refused and test_a_pack_entry_whose_name_disagrees_with_the_registry_is_refused and test_a_pack_entry_description_is_prose_and_is_not_verified.

Obligation J-3 covers what does not change. Writing a pack still writes the current format from the built in registry, and a pack this tool writes loads exactly as it did, and a hand built version 1 pack carrying the original twelve registry entries still loads. The committed examples do not move, because this slice changes only what the loader accepts and the pack this tool writes is registry data, so every committed example regenerates byte identical through the command line. Proven by test_seed_pack_round_trip and test_seed_pack_via_cli and test_version_one_pack_still_loads_the_original_twelve and test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical.


## Round 5 review fixes

The Round 5 red team approved the slice and the two axis review recorded two Minor wording and pinning points, and this section closes them. No behaviour changes in this pass.

The two refusal tests under J-2 now match the full refusal text, naming the entry and the field, where they previously matched only the shared middle of the message, so a later change that dropped the entry id or the field name from that refusal would fail the tests the obligation names. The wording of J-1 is clarified in place in this pass. The set comparison runs last over the rules that already stood before this slice, and the definition comparison of J-2 runs after it, because that comparison indexes the registry by entry id and can only run over a pack whose ids are known to be the version's own.

One reading of FIX2-3 is settled here. The field rules of FIX2-3 and FIX3-6 govern the four fields the format defines, and an entry carrying further fields beyond those four holds unverified data in them, of any shape, exactly as the description limit of J-2 holds unverified prose in a defined field. A reader who gave the FIX2-3 sentence about entry fields its broadest reading would expect an empty extra field to refuse, and this sentence records that the narrow reading governs, so the loader's silence on extra fields is a decision and not a discovery.

## Slice 7 obligations

Slice 7 grows the seed registry a second time, and the pack grows a third format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside because the registry already holds them, and the reasons belong in this record the way the slice 5 record holds its two. A dropped return value was set aside because seed-01 already drops the value from a return, so a second seed would have planted a family the registry holds. A shifted range bound was set aside because seed-10 already raises the last bound of a range call by one, which is the candidate's own planting whatever words name it. The four families taken are the widened exception handler, the swapped dictionary entry, the inverted truth test, and the reassigned loop variable, in that order, taking the ids seed-16 through seed-19. The inverted truth test needed one scoping judgement, and it is recorded here. Seed-14 swaps the connective inside a compound condition and never adds or removes a negation around a whole test, so the two never plant the same change there. Seed-08 is the nearer neighbour, because it wraps an if test in a negation, and the new seed never plants that shape. It strips a negation from a test that already carries one, and it toggles the test of a while loop both ways, and those are shapes seed-08 never plants. The reassigned loop variable needed one small extension of the machinery, because its replacement name is decided by the scope that encloses the loop rather than by the loop node alone, and obligation K-5 states that reading. The obligations below amend I-1 and I-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation K-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds twenty seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-19. The original sixteen seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends I-1, which pinned the registry at exactly sixteen. Proven by test_registry_has_twenty_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation K-2 covers seed-16, widened exception handler. The seed plants at every exception handler that names a type other than the broad base by replacing the named type with the base error type Exception. A bare handler names no type and already catches everything a handler can catch, so it is never planted, and a handler already naming Exception or BaseException is never planted, because the exchange would change nothing. A handler written to recover from one error now catches errors it was never written for and runs its recovery path on them, so a failure surfaces as a wrong result far from its cause while the handler body reads exactly as it always did. This seed is distinct from seed-04, which replaces the body of a handler with a silent pass and never touches the type the handler names. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed and each is planted exactly once in one pass, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Proven by test_seed_sixteen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_sixteen_widens_the_type_and_keeps_the_body and test_seed_sixteen_not_applicable_without_a_typed_handler.

Obligation K-3 covers seed-17, swapped dictionary entry. The seed plants at every dictionary literal that carries at least one keyed entry by exchanging the key and the value of every keyed entry in that literal. A literal carrying entries and read back through them now answers the value where the key was asked and loses the mapping the code wrote, while the literal still reads as an ordinary mapping of the same size. An unpacking entry carries no key, so it has nothing to trade places with and is left exactly as it stands. This seed is distinct from seed-09, which removes the first element of a list or dictionary and never rearranges the entries that stay. A keyed entry that maps an expression to itself exchanges into itself, and the tree change rule of FIX3-2 decides whether a planting counts, exactly as it does for every other seed. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as K-2. Proven by test_seed_seventeen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_seventeen_swaps_every_keyed_entry and test_seed_seventeen_leaves_an_unpacking_entry_standing and test_seed_seventeen_not_applicable_without_a_dictionary.

Obligation K-4 covers seed-18, inverted truth test. The seed toggles the outermost negation of a condition test on the shapes no other seed plants. It plants at every while loop, wrapping its test in a negation when the test carries none and removing the negation when the test carries one, and it plants at every if statement and every conditional expression whose test already carries a negation by removing that negation. An if test that carries no negation is never planted by this seed, because seed-08 owns that shape and already wraps it. A loop now runs while the condition it was written against is false, or a guard written to reject one case admits it instead, and in both readings every comparison inside the test stays exactly right while the sense of the whole test is backwards. This seed is distinct from seed-14, which swaps the connective inside a compound condition, because this seed never touches a connective and plants only on the negation the whole test carries. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as K-2. Proven by test_seed_eighteen_plants_and_is_caught_on_a_loop and test_seed_eighteen_plants_and_is_caught_on_a_negated_guard and test_seed_eighteen_not_applicable_without_its_shapes.

Obligation K-5 covers seed-19, reassigned loop variable. The seed plants at every for loop whose target is one plain name by pointing the loop at a different name already bound in the scope that encloses the loop. The replacement is the first other name bound in that scope, counting the parameters of an enclosing function first and then the names the body of the scope binds in source order. A name counts as bound in a scope when an assignment binds it, or an import binds it, or a with item binds it, or an exception handler names it, or another loop target binds it, or a function or class definition in the body binds it, and the reading never crosses into a nested function or class body and never counts a name bound only inside a comprehension, and it counts the parameters of a function in the order the signature declares them, and a name bound by a walrus inside a comprehension is never counted either, because the reading does not descend into a comprehension at all, even though the language binds that name in the scope holding the comprehension. A loop in a scope that binds no other name is never planted. The loop now writes each item into the wrong variable, so the variable the code reads after the loop keeps whatever it held before it and the variable the loop overwrote loses its own value, and both wrongs surface only after the loop ends. The matches and their replacement names are collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Proven by test_seed_nineteen_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_nineteen_points_the_loop_at_a_body_name and test_seed_nineteen_not_applicable_when_no_other_name_is_bound.

Obligation K-6 covers the third pack format. Writing a seed pack now writes the format canaryfield-seeds/3 carrying twenty entries, one per seed in the grown registry. The loader accepts all three formats and holds each to its own count, refusing a canaryfield-seeds/1 pack that does not carry exactly twelve entries and a canaryfield-seeds/2 pack that does not carry exactly sixteen and a canaryfield-seeds/3 pack that does not carry exactly twenty, and refusing any other format string the way it always has. The registry pinning of J-1 and J-2 applies to each version against its own slice of the one registry, the first twelve entries for version 1 and the first sixteen for version 2 and all twenty for version 3, and every refusal an earlier obligation promised keeps its message and its place in the order the loader checks them. This obligation amends I-6, which spoke of two formats, and the version ranges stated in J-1 and J-2. Proven by test_version_two_pack_still_loads_the_sixteen and test_a_version_three_pack_with_an_invented_id_is_refused and test_a_version_two_pack_carrying_a_grown_registry_id_is_refused and test_a_pack_whose_count_disagrees_with_version_three_is_refused and test_seed_pack_round_trip and test_seed_pack_via_cli.

Obligation K-7 covers the committed examples under the grown registry. The slice 1 catch table and the two gate tables and the seed pack are regenerated through the command line after the registry change, and each table now carries twenty rows and the pack is a version 3 pack. No verdict on the original sixteen seeds changes on the demo project, because each seed still plants alone in its own copy or branch and a new seed cannot reach into another seed planting. The README status records what the new seeds honestly score on the demo project. This obligation amends the example stability readings of I-7 for this slice the way that obligation amended them before. Proven by test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical and test_two_gate_example_shows_an_honest_disagreement and test_two_gate_run_scores_each_gate_on_its_own_evidence.

## Round 6 review fixes

The Round 6 red team recorded one Major on seed-16 and two Minors on seed-19, and the two axis review added one Major on the pinning of K-5 and one Minor on the pinning of K-4, and this section closes them. Two repairs change code, and the rest are pins and one naming each for K-2 and the slice 7 preamble.

FIX6-1. Seed-16 now reads every name a handler's type carries before it decides the handler is plantable. The skip the K-2 sentence promises, for a handler already naming Exception or BaseException, previously read only a bare name, so a handler whose type is a tuple naming one of them was collected and replaced, an exchange that changes nothing the handler catches where the tuple already holds Exception and narrows the handler where the tuple holds BaseException. The names are now gathered from the bare name form and from every member of a tuple form alike, and a handler naming a broad base in any member is skipped, while a tuple naming only narrower types is still widened whole. Proven by test_seed_sixteen_skips_a_tuple_naming_exception and test_seed_sixteen_skips_a_tuple_naming_base_exception and test_seed_sixteen_widens_a_tuple_of_narrow_types in tests/test_round6_fixes.py.

FIX6-2. The parameters of a function are now counted in the order the signature declares them. The reading previously placed the keyword only parameters ahead of the variable argument parameter, so on a signature mixing the two the first other name could be one the declaration names later. Proven by test_seed_nineteen_parameters_follow_signature_order.

The scope rules of K-5 are pinned at content level in the same file, one observation each. A loop inside a nested function is pointed at a name the inner scope binds and never at a name the outer function binds, by test_seed_nineteen_stays_inside_the_inner_scope. Parameters come before body names, by test_seed_nineteen_parameters_come_before_body_names. A name bound only as a comprehension target is never counted, by test_seed_nineteen_ignores_a_comprehension_target. The conditional expression shape of K-4 is pinned by test_seed_eighteen_strips_a_negated_conditional_expression. The walrus consequence of the comprehension reading is stated in K-5 itself in this pass and pinned by test_seed_nineteen_ignores_a_walrus_inside_a_comprehension, and the slice 7 preamble's name claim for seed-10 is reworded in place to the planting identity that carries it.

## Slice 8 obligations

Slice 8 grows the seed registry a third time, and the pack grows a fourth format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the slice 5 and slice 7 records hold theirs. A raised error swapped for another error type named in the module was set aside because the replacement would be chosen from whatever error types the module happens to name, so one seed would plant a different defect from file to file and could never plant at all in a module naming only the type it raises, and because the reading of exception types it needs is the reading seed-16 already performs on handlers, so the family stands closer to ground the registry holds than the four taken. An off by one slice bound was set aside because a slice stop written as a constant inside a return or a comparison is already planted by seed-03 and a computed bound raised by one is already planted by seed-10 on range calls, so the family is held twice over and the distinct remainder is narrower than the candidate as stated. The four families taken are the swapped call argument order, the altered string literal, the dropped numeric negation, and the swapped loop control, in that order, taking the ids seed-20 through seed-23. The obligations below amend I-1 as amended by K-1 and K-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation L-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds twenty four seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-23. The original twenty seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends K-1, which pinned the registry at exactly twenty. Proven by test_registry_has_twenty_four_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation L-2 covers seed-20, swapped call argument order. The seed plants at every call that carries at least two positional arguments by exchanging the first two of them. Each value now reaches the parameter the other was written for, so a callee that treats its first two inputs differently computes or records the wrong thing while the call still reads as an ordinary call with the right number of arguments. This seed is distinct from seed-07, which drops the last argument of a call and never rearranges the ones that stay. The keyword arguments of a call stand exactly as written, because they sit apart from the positional arguments and bind by name rather than by place. A starred argument standing among the first two positional arguments trades places like any other, because it occupies a positional place in the call. A call whose first two positional arguments are the same expression exchanges into itself, and the tree change rule of FIX3-2 decides whether a planting counts, exactly as it does for every other seed. A call with fewer than two positional arguments is never planted. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed and each is planted exactly once in one pass, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3, so a call nested inside the arguments of another matched call is planted once at its own level and the outer exchange never rescans it. Proven by test_seed_twenty_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_swaps_the_first_two_arguments and test_seed_twenty_leaves_keyword_arguments_standing and test_seed_twenty_trades_a_starred_argument_like_any_other and test_seed_twenty_identical_arguments_exchange_into_themselves and test_seed_twenty_not_applicable_without_a_two_argument_call.

Obligation L-3 covers seed-21, altered string literal. The seed plants at every string constant by replacing its first character, putting the letter x in that place or the letter z when the first character already is x. A string the code compares against or builds a result from no longer holds the text the code wrote, so an equality that should hold fails and a message or key or label the code returns is quietly wrong while the literal still reads as ordinary text. Three shapes are never planted, and each exclusion is a decision stated here. A docstring is never planted, because the first string statement of a module or a function or a class body is documentation rather than behaviour, and changing it plants no defect any gate could catch for a behavioural reason. The literal a version assignment carries whole is never planted, because seed-00 owns that shape and replaces the whole of it with its stale string, so this seed reads only the constants that stand anywhere else. An empty string is never planted, because it carries no first character to replace. A bytes literal is never planted, because this seed works on text. A string constant standing as one fixed part of a format string is planted like any other, because the fixed text of a format string is behaviour the code compares or returns exactly as a plain literal is. This seed is distinct from seed-00, which replaces one whole literal standing in one named place with a fixed stale value, while this seed changes one character of every literal in its scope wherever it stands. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as L-2. Proven by test_seed_twenty_one_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_one_changes_the_first_character and test_seed_twenty_one_leaves_docstrings_standing and test_seed_twenty_one_leaves_the_version_literal_standing and test_seed_twenty_one_alters_the_fixed_text_of_a_format_string and test_seed_twenty_one_not_applicable_without_an_in_scope_literal.

Obligation L-4 covers seed-22, dropped numeric negation. The seed plants at every unary minus by removing it and leaving the expression it negated standing in its place. A value the code wrote as negative or as the opposite of a computed quantity now carries the other sign, so a balance or an offset or a bound lands mirrored from where the code put it while the expression still reads as the same quantity. A negation nested inside another negation loses one level at each planting, because the matches are collected from the tree as parsed with their parents and planted deepest first, so the inner removal never strands the outer one on a detached node. The literal a minus sign builds, the way a negative number is written, is planted like any other negation, because the sign of the written number is exactly what the seed removes. A unary plus is never planted and a logical not is never planted, because neither is a numeric negation and the not belongs to the truth test shapes other seeds own. This seed is distinct from seed-03, which adds one to an integer constant and never touches a sign, and from seed-06, which swaps the operator of a binary operation and never touches a unary one. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as L-2, with the parent aware collection the dropped await seed already uses. Proven by test_seed_twenty_two_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_two_removes_the_minus_and_keeps_the_operand and test_seed_twenty_two_drops_a_nested_negation_once_at_each_level and test_seed_twenty_two_leaves_unary_plus_and_logical_not_standing and test_seed_twenty_two_not_applicable_without_a_negation.

Obligation L-5 covers seed-23, swapped loop control. The seed plants at every break statement standing in the body of a loop by replacing it with a continue statement, and it never plants in the other direction. A loop written to stop when its exit condition fires now skips to its next turn instead, so work the loop was written to leave undone gets done or the loop runs past the point its author chose to stop, while the statement still reads as ordinary loop control. The direction is one way because a break and a continue are not symmetric defects, and a continue turned into a break would end loops early in a way this family does not claim. A break belongs to the loop whose body holds it, so a break inside a nested loop becomes a continue of that inner loop and the outer loop keeps its own control exactly as written. A break standing in the else clause of a loop is never planted, because that clause runs after the loop rather than inside it. A break standing inside a function or class defined within a loop is never planted as a break of that outer loop, because the reading does not cross the boundary into the nested body, and it is planted only when a loop inside that function or class holds it in its own body. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed with their parents before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Proven by test_seed_twenty_three_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_three_turns_a_break_into_a_continue and test_seed_twenty_three_plants_the_inner_loop_break_on_its_own_loop and test_seed_twenty_three_leaves_a_break_outside_a_loop_body_standing and test_seed_twenty_three_not_applicable_without_a_break.

Obligation L-6 covers the fourth pack format. Writing a seed pack now writes the format canaryfield-seeds/4 carrying twenty four entries, one per seed in the grown registry. The loader accepts all four formats and holds each to its own count, refusing a canaryfield-seeds/1 pack that does not carry exactly twelve entries and a canaryfield-seeds/2 pack that does not carry exactly sixteen and a canaryfield-seeds/3 pack that does not carry exactly twenty and a canaryfield-seeds/4 pack that does not carry exactly twenty four, and refusing any other format string the way it always has. The registry pinning of J-1 and J-2 applies to each version against its own slice of the one registry, the first twelve entries for version 1 and the first sixteen for version 2 and the first twenty for version 3 and all twenty four for version 4, and every refusal an earlier obligation promised keeps its message and its place in the order the loader checks them. This obligation amends K-6, which spoke of three formats, and the version ranges stated in J-1 and J-2. Proven by test_version_three_pack_still_loads_the_twenty and test_a_version_four_pack_with_an_invented_id_is_refused and test_a_version_three_pack_carrying_a_grown_registry_id_is_refused and test_a_pack_whose_count_disagrees_with_version_four_is_refused and test_seed_pack_round_trip and test_seed_pack_via_cli.

Obligation L-7 covers the committed examples under the grown registry. The slice 1 catch table and the two gate tables and the seed pack are regenerated through the command line after the registry change, and each table now carries twenty four rows and the pack is a version 4 pack. No verdict on the original twenty seeds changes on the demo project, because each seed still plants alone in its own copy or branch and a new seed cannot reach into another seed planting. The README status records what the new seeds honestly score on the demo project. This obligation amends the example stability readings of K-7 for this slice the way that obligation amended them before. Proven by test_copy_mode_example_regenerates_byte_identical and test_two_gate_example_shows_an_honest_disagreement and test_two_gate_run_scores_each_gate_on_its_own_evidence.

## Round 7 review fixes

The Round 7 red team approved the slice outright, and the two axis review recorded one Minor on pinning, and this section closes it. No behaviour changes in this pass.

Four edge rules of slice 8 gain discriminating content level pins in tests/test_round7_fixes.py. The first two only rule of L-2 is pinned past two arguments, so a call of three positional arguments renders with the third standing where it stood. The empty string exclusion and the bytes exclusion of L-3 are pinned beside a neighbouring literal that plants. The positive half of the corrected sentence of L-5 is pinned, so a break inside a loop inside a function defined within a loop renders as that inner loop's continue.

One phrasing of L-4 is settled here. A doubled minus loses both of its levels in the one apply pass, because the seed plants at every matching shape in the file, the discipline slice 4 settled and I-5 states for nested awaits in its parallel sentence. The phrase about losing one level at each planting names the order the collector works in, deepest first, so no level is stranded by the planting above it, and not a ration of one level per pass.

## Slice 9 obligations

Slice 9 grows the seed registry a fourth time, and the pack grows a fifth format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the slice 5 and slice 7 and slice 8 records hold theirs. An attribute name swapped for a sibling attribute of the same object was set aside because the registry holds no reading that can decide siblinghood. Two attributes are siblings only when they belong to the one object, and one base name standing in two scopes can stand for two objects while one object can stand under two names, so the decision needs a name resolution the collectors of this registry never perform, and a seed built on matching names alone would plant collisions between unrelated objects and call them siblings. A default argument value altered was set aside because the family as stated is held in its wider half and repeats a held rule in its narrower half. A string default stands inside the scope of seed-21 today and is altered there, because that scope is every string constant standing outside a docstring and outside a version literal, and a numeric default would be planted by the exchange of one integer constant for the next that seed-03 already performs, restricted to the one position of a default, so the distinct remainder of the family is narrower than the candidate as stated. The four families taken are the swapped augmented assignment operator, the identity comparison swapped to equality, the subscript index shifted by one, and the swapped keyword argument values, in that order, taking the ids seed-24 through seed-27. The obligations below amend L-1 and L-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation M-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds twenty eight seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-27. The original twenty four seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends L-1, which pinned the registry at exactly twenty four. Proven by test_registry_has_twenty_eight_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation M-2 covers seed-24, swapped augmented assignment operator. The seed plants at every augmented assignment whose operator is addition or subtraction or multiplication by exchanging that operator for the one the swapped arithmetic seed names for the same operator, so an addition becomes a subtraction and a subtraction becomes an addition and a multiplication becomes an addition. An augmented assignment carrying any other operator is never planted, because the exchange this family shares with seed-06 names no partner for it. A running total or a scaled quantity now moves in the wrong direction or by the wrong operation at every step, while the statement still reads as an ordinary accumulation in exactly the shape the code always had. The target the assignment writes to takes no part in the decision, so an augmented assignment onto a name or an attribute or a subscript plants alike. This seed is distinct from seed-06, which swaps the operator of a binary operation and never visits an augmented assignment, because an augmented assignment is a statement of its own shape and stands outside the binary operation reading entirely, and it is distinct from seed-12, which exchanges the operands of an operation and never touches its operator. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed and each is planted exactly once in one pass, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Proven by test_seed_twenty_four_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_four_swaps_addition_for_subtraction and test_seed_twenty_four_swaps_subtraction_and_multiplication_for_addition and test_seed_twenty_four_plants_every_match_in_one_file and test_seed_twenty_four_plants_onto_a_subscript_target and test_seed_twenty_four_leaves_an_out_of_table_operator_standing and test_seed_twenty_four_not_applicable_without_an_augmented_assignment.

Obligation M-3 covers seed-25, identity comparison swapped to equality. The seed plants at every comparison whose first operator is an identity operator by replacing that operator with the equality operator of the same polarity, so an is becomes an equals and an is not becomes a not equals. A chained comparison carries its operators in a list and only the first stands exchanged, because the reading of a comparison this registry already gives under seed-02 takes the first operator alone, and a comparison whose first operator is an equality operator is never planted even when an identity operator stands later in its chain. Identity asks whether two names stand for one object and equality asks whether two values count as the same, and the two answers part exactly where code leans on sentinels and singletons and interned values, so a guard written to ask the one now asks the other while every operand inside the test stays exactly right. This seed is distinct from seed-02, which flips the first operator to its opposite inside its own family, turning an is into an is not and an equals into a not equals, and never exchanges one family for the other. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as M-2. Proven by test_seed_twenty_five_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_twenty_five_turns_is_into_equality and test_seed_twenty_five_turns_is_not_into_inequality and test_seed_twenty_five_exchanges_only_the_first_operator_of_a_chain and test_seed_twenty_five_leaves_a_chain_led_by_equality_standing and test_seed_twenty_five_not_applicable_without_an_identity_test.

Obligation M-4 covers seed-26, subscript index shifted by one. The seed plants at every subscript whose whole index is one integer constant by adding one to that constant. The index must stand alone as the whole index expression of the subscript, and the shapes that fail that reading are never planted, each for its own stated reason. A negative index is never planted, because it stands in the tree as a negation over a constant rather than as a constant, and its sign is the shape of the index rather than a value this family shifts. A slice is never planted, because a slice carries bounds and no one constant stands as its index. A tuple index is never planted, because no single constant stands as the whole index of it. A boolean index is never planted, because a boolean standing in the index place is a flag and not a counted position. A name or a computed expression standing as an index is never planted, because this family shifts only a constant the code wrote. Code that reaches for the element at one counted position now reaches for the element beside it, while the subscript still reads as an ordinary index into the same collection. A subscript nested inside another subscript plants at each level, because the index of the inner subscript and the index of the outer subscript are each one constant standing in its own subscript. This seed is distinct from seed-03, which adds one to an integer constant standing inside a return or a comparison and reaches a subscript index only when the subscript stands inside one of those two shapes, and never plants the index of a subscript standing in an assignment or a statement or a call argument, and it is distinct from seed-10, which raises the computed last bound of a range call, a call shape, and never touches an index standing in a subscript, and it is distinct from seed-09, which removes an element from a list or dictionary literal and never touches a subscript reading one. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as M-2. Obligation M-5 covers seed-27, swapped keyword argument values. The seed plants at every call that carries at least two keyword arguments with names by exchanging the values of the first two named keyword arguments in the order they stand in the call. The names stay exactly where they stand and only the values trade places, so each value now binds to the parameter the other was written for, while the call still reads as an ordinary call naming its arguments and carrying the right number of them. The third and later named keyword arguments keep their values, because the exchange this family makes is the exchange of the first two, the same reach seed-20 gives its positional family. An unnamed keyword argument, the double starred expansion a call spreads into its arguments, never takes part and never counts toward the two, because it carries no name a value could bind against, and it stands exactly where it stood even when it stands between the two named arguments whose values trade places. A call whose first two named keyword values are the same expression exchanges into itself, and the tree change rule of FIX3-2 decides whether a planting counts, exactly as it does for every other seed. A call nested inside the value of a keyword argument of another matched call is planted once at its own level, and the outer exchange never rescans it. This seed is distinct from seed-20, which exchanges the first two positional arguments of a call and states that its keyword arguments stand exactly as written, and from seed-07, which drops the last positional argument of a call and never rearranges the ones that stay. ## Round 8 review fixes

The Round 8 two axis review approved the spec axis and recorded three Minors on the standards axis, and the red team approved the behaviour outright and recorded one of the same Minors, and this section closes all of them.

The arithmetic exchange table is now one holding. Seed-24 restated the exchange seed-06 names as a second literal with the same entries, so nothing tied the two together while M-2 worded the exchange as the one the swapped arithmetic seed names. The table now stands once at module level as _ARITHMETIC_SWAPS and both apply functions read it, so the contract sentence is true of the code.

Two edge rules gain their pins in tests/test_round8_fixes.py. An augmented assignment onto an attribute target plants under seed-24, pinned in rendered output. A computed expression standing as a subscript index stands under seed-26, pinned the same way. Both behaviours were already correct and are now proven rather than inspected.

One slice 2 era bullet in the README named the twenty four seed registry as the registry the two gate disagreements stand under, one growth behind the tree. The phrase now names the twenty eight seed registry, the treatment the Round 4 fix pass gave the same lines when their counts moved.
## Slice 10 obligations

Slice 10 grows the seed registry a fifth time, and the pack grows a sixth format version to hold it. Five candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and one of them was set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the earlier records hold theirs. A boolean literal flipped where it stands as a whole assignment value or a whole return value was set aside because seed-05 already flips every boolean literal wherever it stands in the tree, so the candidate would plant a strict subset of a family the registry holds whole, and a narrower copy of a held family is not a new family. Two families return after earlier rounds set them aside, and each returns because the ground that set it aside is now answered in writing. A raised error swapped for another error type named in the module was set aside in slice 8 because the replacement would be chosen from whatever error types the module happens to name, so one seed would plant a different defect from file to file. Obligation N-2 makes that choice a stated rule of the seed rather than an accident of the file, because the first other type name in a stated order is as decidable as any other shape in this registry, and the defect family is the same in every file even though the two names differ. An attribute name swapped for a sibling attribute of the same object was set aside in slice 9 because two attributes are siblings only when they belong to the one object, and one base name standing in two scopes can stand for two objects while one object can stand under two names, so the decision seemed to need a name resolution the collectors never perform. Obligation N-5 answers that ground by never crossing a scope boundary and never reasoning across names. Inside one scope a base name that stands as a parameter the scope body never rebinds, or that the scope binds with exactly one store and never again, denotes the one object it was bound to at every attribute load in that scope, so the attributes loaded off it there are siblings by that reading, and the registry already reasons lexically about the scope that encloses a shape in exactly this way for seed-19. What the rule never claims is that an object standing under two names is one base, or that a base in one scope is the base of the same spelling in another scope, and those are the claims the earlier ground rightly refused. The four families taken are the swapped raised error, the shifted slice stop, the shifted numeric default, and the swapped sibling attribute, in that order, taking the ids seed-28 through seed-31. The obligations below amend M-1 and M-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation N-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds thirty two seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-31. The original twenty eight seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends M-1, which pinned the registry at exactly twenty eight. Proven by test_registry_has_thirty_two_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation N-2 covers seed-28, swapped raised error. The seed plants at every raise whose thrown expression is a call to a name written with a capital first letter the way exception types are written, exchanging the name the call throws for the first other exception type name the module names. The names the module names are gathered in source order from the types its handlers name, read the way seed-16 reads them as a bare name or as the members of a tuple, and from the names its counted raises throw, each name counted once at the place it first stands. The replacement for a raise is the first name in that order that is not the name the raise carries, so a module naming two types plants each of its counted raises toward the head of its own order, and a raise is never planted when the module names no other type. Only the name the call throws changes, and the arguments of the call and any cause the raise chains from stand exactly as written. A bare raise names nothing and is never planted. A raise of a bare name is never planted, because a name standing alone as the thrown expression can stand for an instance as readily as for a type and the reading cannot tell the two apart, and a raise of a call to a name written in lower case is never planted, because such a call builds the value it throws in a factory and names no type, and a raise of a call whose called expression is not a bare name is never planted, because that call names no type the reading can take. This seed is distinct from seed-16, which replaces the type a handler names and never touches a raise, and from seed-04, which replaces the body of a handler and so removes whatever the body held without ever exchanging the type a raise names. The matches and their replacement names are collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Obligation N-3 covers seed-29, shifted slice stop. The seed plants at every slice whose stop is one whole integer constant by adding one to that constant, so a subscript takes one element more than the slice it wrote. Only the stop changes, and the start and the step of the slice stand exactly as written. A negative stop is never planted, because it stands in the tree as a negation over a constant rather than as a constant, and its sign is the shape of the bound rather than a value this family shifts. A computed stop is never planted, because this family shifts only a constant the code wrote, and a boolean stop is never planted, because a boolean standing in the stop place is a flag and not a counted bound, and a slice carrying no stop has nothing to shift. A slice standing inside a tuple index is planted the same way, because its stop is a slice stop of that subscript whatever stands beside it. This seed is distinct from seed-26, which adds one to an index that stands alone as the whole index of a subscript and never plants a slice, because a subscript whose index is a slice carries a slice standing there rather than a constant, and it is distinct from seed-03, which adds one to an integer constant standing inside a return or a comparison and reaches a slice stop only when the subscript stands inside one of those two shapes and never plants the stop of a slice standing in an assignment or a statement, and it is distinct from seed-10, which raises the computed last bound of a range call, a call shape, and never touches a bound standing in a slice. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as N-2.
