Obligation N-4 covers seed-30, shifted numeric default. The seed plants at every numeric default value in a function definition by adding one to that value, so a call that leaves the argument out runs with a value the code never wrote. Positional defaults and keyword only defaults plant alike, and the defaults of a lambda plant the same way, because a lambda carries its defaults in the same place a function does. A required keyword only argument carries no default entry and has nothing to plant. A boolean default is never planted, because the shared integer reading this registry uses refuses booleans by name, since a boolean standing as a default is a flag rather than a counted value, and a negative default is never planted, because it stands in the tree as a negation over a constant rather than as a constant, and a string default is never planted by this seed, because seed-21 already plants it, and a float default is never planted, because the shared integer reading reaches only whole values and a float stands outside it the way a boolean does. This seed is distinct from seed-03, which adds one to an integer constant standing inside a return or a comparison and never reaches a default standing in a definition, and it is distinct from seed-21, which changes the first character of every string constant in its scope and so reaches a string default while never touching a numeric one. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as N-2. Proven by test_seed_thirty_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_thirty_shifts_a_positional_default_by_one and test_seed_thirty_shifts_a_keyword_only_default and test_seed_thirty_plants_a_lambda_default and test_seed_thirty_leaves_a_boolean_default_standing and test_seed_thirty_leaves_a_negative_default_standing and test_seed_thirty_leaves_a_string_default_standing and test_seed_thirty_not_applicable_without_a_numeric_default.

Obligation N-5 covers seed-31, swapped sibling attribute. The seed reads each scope on its own statements, where a scope is the module or the body of one function or class or lambda, and it never carries a reading across the boundary into a nested scope, because a nested scope is judged on its own statements when the walk reaches it, and the header expressions of a nested definition, its decorators and argument defaults and annotations and a class definition's bases, stand in the enclosing scope where they are written and are judged there, with only the body standing inside the nested scope, as the Round 9 review fixes record. In one scope, a base name qualifies when it stands among the parameters of that scope and no store of the name stands in the scope body, or when exactly one store of the name stands in the scope body. A store is a name standing in a store position in the scope's own statements, and the reading descends through the other statements of the scope, comprehensions among them, stopping only at a nested scope. Where a qualified base carries at least two distinct attribute names among its loads in that scope, the seed plants at every load of the first of those names in source order by replacing it with the second, so the value is read off a different part of the one object than the code named while the expression still reads as an ordinary attribute read. An attribute standing in a store position is never planted, because this family swaps what a scope reads and a write names the part it means to change. A base the scope rebinds is never planted on, because once the name is bound a second time the two loads can read two objects and the sibling claim fails, and a base carrying only one distinct loaded attribute name has nothing to trade with. This seed is distinct from every seed before it, because no earlier apply function reads an attribute name at all, and it is nearest to seed-19 in method, which also decides a planting from the names one scope binds, though seed-19 points a loop at a bound name and never touches an attribute. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. ## Round 9 review fixes

The Round 9 red team returned CONDITIONAL with two Minors and the two axis review returned APPROVE on Axis A and CONDITIONAL with one Minor on Axis B, and this section closes all three.

The red team showed that the seed-31 walk changed scope at the nested definition itself, so a nested definition's header expressions were gathered into the nested scope's record even though they stand in the enclosing scope and are evaluated there, and a planting could rest on evidence from the wrong side of the boundary N-5 says is never crossed. The walk now judges a nested definition's decorators and argument defaults and annotations and a class definition's bases in the enclosing record and only its body in its own, and obligation N-5 states that reading in its scope sentence. Both directions are pinned in tests/test_round9_fixes.py, the enclosing scope planting on a load standing in a nested default, and no planting standing where the only evidence for the swap lived inside the nested body.

The standards axis found the comprehension descent clause of N-5 carried no pin, though its own probes showed the behaviour follows the words. The same test file now pins both directions, a store standing as a comprehension target disqualifying its base in the enclosing scope, and a load inside a comprehension counting as a load of that scope.

The red team also found seed-30's words said numeric without qualification while float defaults stand outside the shared integer reading the seed follows. Obligation N-4 now names float defaults among the shapes that stand, beside the boolean and negative and string shapes it already named. No behaviour changes on that point.

## Slice 11 obligations

Slice 11 grows the seed registry a sixth time, and the pack grows a seventh format version to hold it. Five candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and one of them was set aside, and the reason belongs in this record the way the earlier records hold theirs. A flipped range step sign was set aside because the first four candidates each survived judgement against the apply functions the registry already holds, so the reserve was never needed, and a family held in reserve is worth more unplanted than a narrower family planted to fill a count. The four families taken are the narrowed exception handler, the swapped parameter defaults, the dropped finally body, and the dropped decorator, in that order, taking the ids seed-32 through seed-35. The narrowed exception handler is the mirror of the widened one and never plants where that one plants, because seed-16 widens a handler that names a narrower type toward the broad base and this seed narrows a handler that names the broad base toward a type its own try body raises, and a handler stands in exactly one of those two readings at a time. The swapped parameter defaults exchange two values standing in one definition and never touch a call, because seed-20 exchanges values at a call site and seed-27 exchanges keyword values at a call site and seed-30 raises one default by one without ever exchanging two. The dropped finally body removes the statements of a finally block and nothing else, because seed-13 removes the else branch of an if statement and seed-04 replaces the body of an exception handler, and no seed before this one reads the finally block of a try statement at all. The dropped decorator removes one entry from a decorator list, and no seed before this one reads a decorator list, so the family stands on ground the registry has never planted. The obligations below amend N-1 and N-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation O-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds thirty six seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-35. The original thirty two seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends N-1, which pinned the registry at exactly thirty two. Proven by test_registry_has_thirty_six_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation O-2 covers seed-32, narrowed exception handler. The seed plants at every exception handler whose type names the broad base Exception or BaseException, in the bare name form or in any member of a tuple form, read the way seed-16 and FIX6-1 read the names a handler carries, by replacing the whole type the handler names with the first specific exception type the body of the same try statement raises. The replacement is the first name in source order that a counted raise in that try body throws, where a counted raise is the raise shape slice 10 settled, a raise whose thrown expression is a call to a name written with a capital first letter, and a raise of the broad base itself never serves as the replacement, because narrowing a handler toward the base it already names would change nothing. The raises are gathered from the statements of the try body and the reading stops at any nested function or class or lambda, because a raise inside one of those belongs to that nested scope and never propagates through this try statement when the body runs, while a raise standing inside a try statement nested in the body is gathered like any other statement of the body, the lexical reading this registry gives every shape it plants. A handler naming no broad base is never planted, and a bare handler is never planted, because it names no type and already catches everything a handler can catch, the same ground seed-16 gives for leaving it standing. A try body that raises no counted specific type leaves its handlers standing, because there is no narrower name the reading can honestly choose. The name a handler narrows to is one its own try body raises, so the failure written to be recovered from still reaches the handler when its type is the chosen one, while every other failure the broad handler was written to absorb now escapes past it to the caller. This seed is distinct from seed-16, which widens a handler toward the broad base and never narrows one, and from seed-04, which replaces the body of a handler and never touches the type it names, and from seed-28, which exchanges the type a raise throws and never touches the type a handler names. The matches and their replacement names are collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Obligation O-3 covers seed-33, swapped parameter defaults. The seed plants at every function definition and every lambda whose arguments carry at least two default values by exchanging the first two of those values, so each of the two parameters falls back to the value the code wrote for the other. The defaults of one definition stand in one pool in the order the signature declares them, the positional defaults first in their declared order and then the keyword only defaults in theirs, and a keyword only argument that carries no default entry stands in no pool and never counts toward the two. The values trade places whole, whatever expressions they are, because a default is a value the definition carries and the exchange this family makes reads no type and no literal form, and the third and later defaults keep their values exactly as written. A definition carrying a single default is never planted, because there is no second value for its default to trade with. Two defaults holding the same expression exchange into themselves, and the tree change rule of FIX3-2 decides whether a planting counts, exactly as it does for every other seed. This seed is distinct from seed-30, which raises one integer default by one and never exchanges two values, and from seed-20 and seed-27, which exchange values at a call site and never touch the defaults a definition carries. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as O-2. Obligation O-4 covers seed-34, dropped finally body. The seed plants at every try statement that carries a finally block by removing the whole finally block, leaving the body of the try and its handlers and its else clause standing exactly as written. The statements a finally block holds are the cleanup the code wrote to run on every path out of the try, on the return path and on the raising path alike, so removing the block means the handle is never closed and the lock is never released and the counter is never settled, while the try reads as ordinary guarded code in exactly the shape it always had. A try statement carrying no finally block is never planted, because it holds nothing this family removes. The star form of a try statement, the one whose handlers are written with an except star, carries its finally block in the same place and plants the same way. This seed is distinct from seed-13, which removes the else branch of an if statement and never touches a try statement, and from seed-04, which replaces the body of an exception handler with a silent pass and leaves a finally block standing, and from seed-32, which exchanges the type a handler of the same try statement names and never removes a statement. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as O-2. Obligation O-5 covers seed-35, dropped decorator. The seed plants at every function definition and every async function definition and every class definition that carries at least one decorator by removing the first decorator in its list, the one written highest above the definition. ## Slice 12 obligations

Slice 12 grows the seed registry a seventh time, and the pack grows an eighth format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the earlier records hold theirs. Swapped branch bodies of an if statement were set aside because seed-08 already negates the test of every if statement, and exchanging the body and the else branch of an if statement admits and rejects exactly the cases that negation admits and rejects, so the family is already planted in effect whatever spelling carries it. A shifted slice start was set aside because the family stands between two readings the registry already holds and the distinct remainder is narrower than the candidate as stated, since a constant start standing inside a return or a comparison is already planted by seed-03 and the slice node already carries seed-26 on its whole index form and seed-29 on its stop, so a third planting on that one node crowds one shape while whole families stand unplanted elsewhere. The four families taken are the dropped exception handler, the dropped yield, the dropped condition term, and the dropped base class, in that order, taking the ids seed-36 through seed-39. The dropped exception handler removes a member of a handler list and never touches what a handler names or what its body holds, because seed-16 and seed-32 exchange the type a handler names and seed-04 replaces the body a handler holds, and no seed before this one changes which handlers a try statement carries. The dropped yield is the mirror of the dropped await and stands on ground no seed has planted, because seed-15 replaces an await with the value it awaited and no apply function in the registry reads a yield at all. The dropped condition term removes one term of a compound condition and never touches its operator, because seed-14 exchanges the operator of every boolean operation while every term stands, and seed-08 and seed-18 work on negations of whole tests and never remove a term. The dropped base class removes a member of the base list of a class definition, ground no seed has planted, because seed-35 removes a member of a decorator list and leaves every base standing, and seed-31 reads the bases of a class only as header evidence of the enclosing scope and never plants on a definition, and seed-09 removes the first element of a list or dictionary literal and a base list is neither. Obligation P-2 covers seed-36, dropped exception handler. The seed plants at every try statement whose handler list can lose its first handler and stay a whole try statement, by removing the first handler in the order the handlers stand. A try statement can lose its first handler and stay whole when it carries at least two handlers, or when it carries a finally block beside its one handler, because a try statement with no handler and no finally block is not a shape the language accepts, and a try statement carrying one handler and an else clause and no finally block is therefore never planted, since removing its handler would leave the else clause standing over no handler at all. A try statement carrying no handlers is never planted, because it holds nothing this family removes. The star form of a try statement carries its handlers in the same place and plants the same way. The handlers that stay keep their places and their order, and the body of the try and its else clause and its finally block stand exactly as written. The failure the removed handler was written to recover from now escapes past the try to the caller, or reaches a later handler written for a different failure, while the try reads as ordinary guarded code in exactly the shape it always had. Obligation P-3 covers seed-37, dropped yield. ## Round 11 review fixes

The red team rejected slice 12 on two Criticals of one kind, and the two axis review found the first of them independently as a Major. A planting must never leave a tree the language refuses, because the engine writes planted trees with no compile check, so a broken planting scores as a catch against a gate that was never given the documented defect to judge. That sentence now stands as an invariant over the whole registry, and it is pinned for every seed at once by test_every_seed_plants_only_compiling_trees_on_the_repo_corpus in tests/test_round11_fixes.py, which applies every seed to every Python file in this repo and compiles every planted output.

P-2 is amended at its boundary. The lone handler case of seed-36 stands plantable beside a finally block only when no else clause stands. A lone handler standing beside both an else clause and a finally block is never planted, because removing the handler would leave an else clause standing over no handler at all, a shape the language refuses for the same reason P-2 already refuses the else clause with no finally. The built code planted that conjunction, and the conjunction is pinned both ways by test_seed_thirty_six_refuses_a_lone_handler_beside_an_else_and_a_finally and its star form beside it, each failing on the code as built.

O-4 is amended at its handlerless edge, and the catch it shipped with is corrected on the record. As built in slice 11, seed-34 emptied the finally body of every try that carries one, and on a try and finally with no handler that left a bare try block the language refuses. The seed's own caught fixture is a fetch guarded by try and finally alone, so the catch recorded for seed-34 in slice 11 could not tell the defect from the breakage, and it is withdrawn as evidence. The planting now removes the whole finally block where a handler stands, exactly as O-4 always stated, and where no handler stands it replaces the finally body with a pass, so the tree stays a shape the language accepts while the cleanup the finally block held is gone in both forms. The catch is re-earned against a compiling planted copy by test_seed_thirty_four_catch_is_earned_on_a_compiling_copy, which compiles the planted file before it trusts the verdict, and the two plantings are pinned by test_seed_thirty_four_plants_a_handlerless_finally_as_a_pass_body and test_seed_thirty_four_still_removes_the_block_beside_handlers, each failing on the code as built. No example verdict moves under the repair, because neither example target holds a shape either Critical touched.

The seed-36 registry description is corrected to name both outcomes of the removal, that the failure the removed handler was written to recover from reaches a later handler instead, or escapes to the caller where no handler stands after it, and the seed pack example is regenerated with the corrected text and no other change.

The advisory step 0 review of the round 11 pull request then found the same refusal class in four more places, each reproduced by the owner before any fix. A planting can remove the only binding for a name a surviving nonlocal declaration needs, and the language refuses the result with no binding for the name found. Seed-34 plants it when the finally body holds the only binding, seed-36 when the removed handler holds it, seed-38 when the removed term is a named expression holding it, and seed-39 when the removed base is a named expression holding it. The invariant above is therefore held at the write path in the engine, the one place every planting passes through. A planting whose rendered form does not compile is never written and the file does not count as planted, so no seed needs its own scope repair for the guarantee to hold. The corpus pin above stands for direct plantings over real code, and the write path guarantee is pinned on the four nonlocal shapes, each standing unplanted and unchanged after its seed runs over it.

Slice 13 grows the seed registry an eighth time, and the pack grows a ninth format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the earlier records hold theirs. Swapped conditional expression branches were set aside because exchanging the two values of a conditional expression admits and rejects exactly the cases that negating its test admits and rejects, and seed-08 already negates the test of every if statement while seed-18 already toggles the negation a conditional expression test carries, so the family is already planted in effect on the shapes it would claim, the same ground on which slice 12 set aside the swapped branch bodies of an if statement. Swapped comparison operands were set aside because on the operators whose order decides the answer the exchange is the flip seed-02 already plants with its operands standing in the other order, and on the equality family the exchange changes nothing the expression decides, so the candidate stands between a held effect and an empty one and is a new family in neither reading. A shifted slice start was weighed in slice 12 and set aside there because a constant start standing inside a return or a comparison is already planted by seed-03 and the slice node already carried seed-26 on its whole index form and seed-29 on its stop, and it is taken now for the remainder that reasoning conceded, the start of a slice standing where the return and comparison reading of seed-03 never reaches, because that ground is whole and unplanted and the reading is the one the stop already established on the same node. The reversed loop control takes the direction seed-23 stated it would never plant, and it takes that direction as a family of its own rather than as a widening of seed-23, because seed-23 gave its reason in its own text, that the two directions are not symmetric defects, and a loop that ends at the first item it was written to skip loses every later item as surely as a loop that runs on past its stop does work its author chose to leave undone. The dropped scope declaration removes a statement that declares where a name binds and never removes a binding itself, so the refusal class the Round 11 review fixes recorded, a planting that removes the only binding for a surviving nonlocal declaration, cannot arise from this planting, and the one shape that could still leave a refused tree, a statement list emptied by the removal, is refused by the guard Q-4 states. The four families taken are the shifted slice start, the reversed loop control, the dropped scope declaration, and the swapped assignment values, in that order, taking the ids seed-40 through seed-43. Obligation Q-2 covers seed-40, shifted slice start. The seed plants at every slice whose start is one whole integer constant by adding one to that constant, so a subscript takes its elements from one position later than the slice it wrote and the first element it was written to include is silently left behind. A computed start is never planted, because this family shifts only a constant the code wrote, and a boolean start is never planted, because a boolean standing in the start place is a flag and not a counted bound, and a slice carrying no start has nothing to shift. A slice standing inside a tuple index is planted the same way, because its start is a slice start of that subscript whatever stands beside it. ## Round 12 review fixes

The two axis review and the red team returned the same three record gaps at the edges of the new seeds, and two further edges that carried no pin, and the fixes below close them without changing any behaviour the probes showed to be sound.

The first gap is the ground Q-2 states against seed-03, and the preamble's framing of seed-40 as planting the remainder a broader family never reaches. On a slice whose start stands alone, with no stop, inside a return or a comparison, seed-03 and seed-40 plant the identical tree, because seed-03 moves the start constant as the constant of that return or comparison and seed-40 moves it as the slice start and the two readings land on the same node, and the slice preamble's picture of disjoint ground does not hold on that conjunction. The two seeds diverge where a stop stands, because seed-03 moves the stop as well and seed-40 never touches it, and they diverge on every slice standing outside a return or a comparison, which seed-03 never reaches. The distinctness sentence of Q-2 is amended by this statement to that reading, and the overlap and the divergence are pinned in tests/test_round12_fixes.py.

The second gap is a boundary Q-3 never named that L-5 states for seed-23. A continue standing in the else clause of a loop is never planted, because that clause runs after the loop rather than inside it, and a continue standing in the else clause of an inner loop, which is a control of the outer loop standing in its body, is never planted either, because this family plants no else clause of any loop, the same discipline seed-23 holds for a break. Q-3 is amended by this statement to that reading, and both shapes are pinned in tests/test_round12_fixes.py.

The third gap is a shape Q-5 never named. An augmented assignment is a statement of its own shape and not an assignment in the reading Q-5 states, so seed-43 never plants at one even when its value is a tuple, and Q-5 is amended by this statement to say so, with the shape pinned in tests/test_round12_fixes.py.

The two unpinned edges are pinned in the same file. The emptied list guard of Q-4 holds in a handler body and in a finally body, where a declaration standing alone is refused and a declaration standing beside another statement is planted, and the named expression conjunction of Q-5 holds in both places, where a named expression standing first or second among the exchanged elements trades places under the swap and the planted tree still compiles.

The last correction is to words rather than to behaviour. The registry description of seed-42 pictured every planted outcome as a silent local write, but an augmented assignment in the planted function reads the local before it binds and raises UnboundLocalError where a plain assignment lands silently, and the gate catches the planting either way. The description now names both outcomes, and the seed pack under examples is regenerated with the corrected line and no other change.

The advisory step 0 review of the round 12 pull request then narrowed the first correction above at two edges, and both narrowings are recorded here because the record should hold the claim at the width the probes prove. The identity between the two plantings holds exactly where the slice start is the only integer constant seed-03 reaches in the enclosing return or comparison, and it parts where another constant stands in that expression, as a tuple sibling beside the subscript stands, because seed-03 moves every constant of the return or comparison and seed-40 moves the start alone. And seed-03 reaches a slice start standing in a call argument when the call itself stands inside a return or a comparison, so the never of Q-2's distinctness sentence holds for a call argument standing in an assignment or a bare statement and not for one standing inside those two shapes. Both edges are pinned in tests/test_round12_fixes.py beside the pins of the first correction.

## Slice 14 obligations

Slice 14 grows the seed registry a ninth time, and the pack grows a tenth format version to hold it. Six candidate families were weighed for the four new seeds, and two of them were set aside, and the reasons belong in this record the way the earlier records hold theirs. Swapped dictionary entry values, exchanging the values of the first two keyed entries of a dictionary literal, was set aside because the dictionary literal already carries two plantings, seed-09 removing its first entry and seed-17 exchanging the key and the value inside every entry, and a third planting on that one node crowds it while whole families stand unplanted elsewhere, the ground slice 12 stated for the slice node. A dropped try else clause was set aside because the try statement already carries plantings on the bodies of its handlers and on the types its handlers name and on its finally block and on its handler list, while no seed in the registry reads the else clause of a loop at all, so the loop clause was taken and the try clause left standing. The flipped range step sign returns from the reserve slice 11 recorded, and its scope is settled here by probe rather than left to a reading a later round would have to repair. The probes were run against the planting code as it stands at forty four seeds, and their findings stand in this preamble. Seed-10 wraps the last argument of a range call in an addition of one whatever position that argument stands in, so on a call of three arguments it plants the step as an addition and never as a negation, and a negated constant step renders as a plain negative literal and compiles, and a step expression wrapped in a unary minus compiles, while a starred step wrapped in one renders a shape the language refuses, so the starred form is excluded by the seed itself and never left to the engine guard alone. A step already carrying a written minus is planted by seed-22, which removes the minus, so this seed never collects one. Removing the else clause of a for loop or a while loop or an async for loop compiles on every plain shape the probes tried, and the one conjunction that refuses is the Round 11 shape, an else clause holding the only binding for a nonlocal declaration standing in a nested function, where the removal leaves the declaration with no binding and the engine write path refuses the planting whole. No seed removes a keyword argument from a call, because seed-07 reads only the positional arguments of a call and seed-27 exchanges the values of two keyword arguments and removes nothing, and a keyword whose value holds the only binding for a nonlocal declaration stands in the same refusal class and is pinned at the write path the same way. No seed removes a logical negation standing outside a condition test, because seed-08 wraps the test of every if statement whole, seed-18 strips or wraps only a negation that is the whole test of an if statement or a while loop or a conditional expression, and seed-14 exchanges a connective and never touches a negation, so a negation standing in a return value or an assignment value or the body of a conditional expression or the test of an assert stands unplanted by all three. The four families taken are the dropped loop else clause, the dropped keyword argument, the flipped range step sign, and the dropped logical negation, in that order, taking the ids seed-44 through seed-47. The obligations below amend Q-1 and Q-6 and the version ranges of J-1 and J-2 where they conflict, and the amendments are stated here rather than left as silent conflicts between the texts.

Obligation R-1 covers the grown registry. The registry holds forty eight seeds with unique stable ids running in order from seed-00 to seed-47. The original forty four seeds keep their ids and their names and defect classes and descriptions and apply behaviour exactly as the earlier slices left them, and the four new seeds take the next four ids. This obligation amends Q-1, which pinned the registry at exactly forty four. Proven by test_registry_has_forty_eight_unique_seeds and test_seed_zero_is_the_stale_version_string and test_seed_metadata_is_complete.

Obligation R-2 covers seed-44, dropped loop else clause. The seed plants at every for loop and async for loop and while loop that carries an else clause by removing the whole clause, leaving the body of the loop and its test and its target standing exactly as written. The else clause of a loop is the path the code wrote for the loop finishing without a break, the not found path of a search or the drained path of a queue, so removing it makes that path silently never run and the code after the loop meets whatever the loop left standing instead. A loop carrying no else clause is never planted, because it holds nothing this family removes. A loop standing in the else clause of another loop is detached with that clause when the outer planting removes it, and the written file shows the outer planting alone, under the reading H-3 gives every seed. This seed is distinct from seed-13, which removes the else branch of an if statement and never reads a loop, and from seed-23 and seed-41, which exchange the loop control statements standing in a loop body and never touch an else clause. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. Proven by test_seed_forty_four_plants_and_is_caught and test_seed_forty_four_drops_the_else_clause_of_a_for_loop and test_seed_forty_four_drops_the_else_clause_of_a_while_loop and test_seed_forty_four_drops_the_else_clause_of_an_async_for_loop and test_seed_forty_four_plants_every_loop_else_in_one_file and test_seed_forty_four_shows_the_outer_planting_where_an_else_clause_holds_a_loop and test_seed_forty_four_plants_a_loop_else_standing_inside_a_try_finally and test_seed_forty_four_planting_that_would_break_a_nonlocal_binding_is_never_written and test_seed_forty_four_leaves_a_loop_without_an_else_clause_standing and test_seed_forty_four_not_applicable_without_a_loop_else.

Obligation R-3 covers seed-45, dropped keyword argument. The seed plants at every call carrying at least one keyword argument with a name by removing the first named keyword argument in the order the keywords stand in the call. The names and values that stay keep their places and their order, and the positional arguments of the call stand exactly as written. The callee now runs without the value the caller named for that parameter, falling back to the default the definition wrote or failing for its absence, while the call still reads as an ordinary call that names its remaining arguments. An unnamed keyword argument, the double starred expansion a call spreads into its arguments, never counts toward the one and is never removed, because it carries no name a value could bind against, and it stands exactly where it stood even when it stands before the named argument this planting removes. A call standing inside the value of the keyword argument this planting removes is changed in memory and is no longer part of the written file, so the written file shows the outer planting alone, under the reading H-3 gives every seed. This seed is distinct from seed-07, which drops the last positional argument of a call carrying at least two and never reads its keyword arguments, and from seed-27, which exchanges the values of the first two named keyword arguments and removes nothing, and from seed-20, which exchanges the first two positional arguments and states that the keyword arguments of a call stand exactly as written. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as R-2. Obligation R-4 covers seed-46, flipped range step sign. The seed plants at every call to the name range carrying exactly three positional arguments by negating the third argument, the step the call carries. A step standing as a whole integer constant is replaced by the constant of the opposite sign, and a step standing as any other expression is wrapped in a unary minus, so the sequence the call produces travels in the opposite direction from the one the code wrote and yields the values standing in that direction, or none where the bounds admit none. A call carrying one or two arguments is never planted, because it carries no step this family can negate and its last argument is a bound, which is the ground seed-10 plants. A step already carrying a written minus is never planted, because seed-22 owns the removal of a written minus wherever one stands and already plants that step by removing it. A step standing as the constant zero is never planted, because negating zero changes nothing the call decides. A boolean step is never planted, because a boolean standing in the step place is a flag and not a counted step, the reading this registry gives a boolean standing as a default in seed-30. A starred step is never planted, because it occupies the step place as an unpacking rather than as one step value, and wrapping an unpacking in a minus renders a shape the language refuses, as the probes recorded, so the exclusion stands in the seed and not only in the engine guard. This seed is distinct from seed-10, which raises the last argument of a range call by one and so plants a call of three arguments by adding one to its step, a different change on every step shape this seed plants, since a constant step stands as an addition of one under that seed and as its opposite under this one and no whole number is both, and an expression step gains an addition there and a negation here. The matches are collected and planted under the same discipline as R-2. Obligation R-5 covers seed-47, dropped logical negation. The seed plants at every unary expression whose operator is a logical not and which does not stand inside the test expression of an if statement or a while loop or a conditional expression, by replacing the negation with the expression it negated. The boundary is the test expression whole, so a negation that is a test and a negation standing anywhere inside one, as one term of a compound test or inside a lambda the test carries, are never planted, because seed-08 wraps the test of every if statement and seed-18 toggles the negation a whole test carries, and the ground inside a test belongs to those two and to the connective exchange of seed-14. A negation nested inside another negation outside a test loses both levels in the one pass, because the matches are collected with their parents and planted deepest first, the discipline the dropped await seed settled. This seed is distinct from seed-22, which removes a unary minus and never reads a logical not, and from seed-05, which flips a boolean literal and never reads an operator. The matches are collected from the tree as parsed with their parents before any change is made, under the discipline of H-2 and H-3. ## Round 13 review fixes

The red team returned one Major and two Minors against slice 14, and the fixes below close them. The two axis review approved both axes, its one Minor of record being the place of the narrowing commit in the branch order, which the third item below answers.

The Major is ground seed-47 planted that its own boundary disowns. R-5 gives the ground inside a condition test to seed-08 and seed-18 and seed-14, and a match case guard is a condition test in the same sense, deciding whether the case runs the way a test decides whether a branch runs, but the enumeration of test holders in R-5 named only the if statement, the while loop, and the conditional expression, and none of the three seeds that own test ground reads a match guard at all, so seed-47 planted a negation standing in a guard and flipped the very kind of test the slice promised to leave standing. R-5 is amended by this statement to that reading. A negation standing anywhere inside the guard of a match case is never planted, and the exclusion follows the guard into everything it carries, the same discipline the test of a statement already holds. The shape is pinned in tests/test_round13_fixes.py, at the guard itself, at a guard of a nested match, and beside a negation outside the match that still plants while the guard negation stands.

The first Minor is a step seed-46 negated into itself. R-4 excludes a step standing as the constant zero because negating zero changes nothing the call decides, but the exclusion as built read only the integer zero, so a step standing as a float zero was wrapped and rendered as its own negative, and a step standing as zero under a unary plus was wrapped the same way, each a planting that changes nothing. R-4 is amended by this statement to the width its reason already claims. The zero exclusion covers every numeric literal spelling of zero, the integer zero and a float zero and a complex zero, and any of them standing under a unary plus. The spellings are pinned in tests/test_round13_fixes.py beside a nonzero float step that still plants.

The second Minor is a matter of record rather than behaviour. The narrowing commit that opens this branch, db5f2c0, changes no code, so the pins it carries for the seed-03 and seed-40 identity cannot fail on the parent code, and they stand as guards holding the corrected statement at its two edges rather than as discrimination. The record says so plainly here rather than counting them among the round's discriminating pins.


## Rust port slice 1 obligations

The ruling of 2026-11-02 makes every repo in the program a Rust library, and this section states the obligations of the first port slice of canaryfield. The landed Python is the behavioral spec for everything ported here and stays in the tree untouched by this slice. The parts whose behavior is data rather than syntax come first, and those parts are the seed registry and the seed pack file the registry is written out as. The seed apply functions rest on the Python ast module, so they are not in this slice, and a hand written parser for the Python source shapes the seeds read is the largest remaining piece of the port and belongs to a later slice that names it. The loader behaviors these obligations rest on were probed against the landed Python before they were written, and the probe findings are these. The bytes the Python writes for the current pack equal the committed example under examples byte for byte. A pack whose entries stand in an order other than registry order loads and returns its entries in the order the document wrote them. An entry carrying a field beyond the four the format names loads with that field standing in the returned entry. A key written twice inside one JSON object resolves to its last value, the reading a Python dict gives the document. The refusal for an entry missing fields carries the Python repr of the entry, so a refused entry is named in the record exactly as it stood in the document.

Obligation PORT-1 covers the crate shape. The repo root carries a Cargo manifest that declares a library crate named canaryfield and one binary target of the same name, on edition 2021, with no dependency outside the Rust standard library. The library is organized as an errors module, a JSON module holding the document reading and writing the pack file needs, a registry module holding the seed registry as data, and a seed pack module holding the pack document and its loader and writer. The binary is a thin shell over the pack writing entry. The continuous integration workflow gains a cargo job that builds the crate and runs its tests beside the standing Python job, and the Python job and its gate command stand unchanged.

Obligation PORT-2 covers the seed registry as data. The Rust registry holds the forty eight seeds in registry order, each with the id and the name and the defect class and the description the Python registry in canaryfield/seeds.py carries for it, and the ten pack formats read their registries from this one table by prefix, the first twelve entries for canaryfield-seeds/1 and the first sixteen for canaryfield-seeds/2 and so on by the counts PORT-4 states up to all forty eight for canaryfield-seeds/10. The apply function each Python seed carries is not part of this table and is not ported in this slice, because an apply function is behavior over a parsed Python tree and the parser it needs is the later piece PORT-6 names.

Obligation PORT-3 covers the pack document and its writing. The Rust pack document for the current registry is a JSON object holding the format string canaryfield-seeds/10 and one entry per seed in registry order, each entry holding exactly the id and the name and the defect class and the description of its seed in that order. Writing a pack renders that document as JSON with a two space indent and one trailing newline and creates the parent directories of the handed over path the way the Python writer does, and the bytes written equal the bytes the Python writer writes for the same registry, the committed example under examples included.

Obligation PORT-4 covers the pack loader. The Rust loader reads a pack file and returns its entries in the order the document wrote them, and it holds each of the ten formats to its own entry count, twelve for canaryfield-seeds/1 and sixteen for canaryfield-seeds/2 and twenty for canaryfield-seeds/3 and twenty four for canaryfield-seeds/4 and twenty eight for canaryfield-seeds/5 and thirty two for canaryfield-seeds/6 and thirty six for canaryfield-seeds/7 and forty for canaryfield-seeds/8 and forty four for canaryfield-seeds/9 and forty eight for canaryfield-seeds/10, and it holds each format to the registry slice of that count. Every refusal the Python loader raises is raised by the Rust loader with the same text and in the same order the Python checks them. A document that is not an object or whose format is none of the ten is refused as not a canaryfield seed pack and names the handed over path. A document whose seed list is missing or is not a list is refused as carrying no seed list and names the path. A pack whose entry count disagrees with the count its format carries is refused with both counts named and the path named. The entries are then read in document order, and an entry that is not an object or lacks any of the four fields is refused as missing fields and carries the Python repr of the entry, and an entry whose id or name or defect class or description is not a piece of text with at least one non whitespace character is refused on the first such field in that field order and carries the repr of the whole entry, and an entry whose id an earlier entry already carried is refused as a duplicate id. The ids of the pack must then be exactly the ids of the registry slice for its format and a pack failing that is refused on its ids, and each entry must then agree with the registry on its name and then on its defect class and a disagreement is refused naming the entry and the field and the path. The description of an entry is checked only for presence and for non emptiness and never against the registry wording, and a field beyond the four rides along in the returned entry, and a top level key beyond the format and the seed list changes nothing. Whether a piece of text holds a non whitespace character is judged by the whitespace reading the Python strip applies. A document text that does not parse as JSON is refused by both loaders, and the wording of that refusal belongs to the JSON decoder of each implementation rather than to this crate, so it stands outside the recorded conformance PORT-6 describes. A format value standing as a list or an object crashes the Python loader with a type error where the Rust loader refuses the document as not a canaryfield seed pack, because a crash is not a reading and is not reproduced. A string escape naming a surrogate that no following escape completes into a pair is read by the Python decoder as a string holding that surrogate, and the Rust text type can hold no such string, so the Rust decoder refuses the document at that escape. That refusal belongs to the decoder of each implementation the way the wording of the malformed text refusal does, and it stands outside the recorded conformance PORT-6 describes on the same ground. A document nested deeper than four thousand levels meets the recursion limit of the Python decoder and raises its recursion error, a crash of the same family and not a reading, where the Rust decoder refuses the document past that depth with its own invalid document wording, so neither loader reads a document nested deeper than four thousand levels. A pack path that cannot be read as text at all, standing missing or standing as a directory or holding bytes that are not UTF-8 text, is refused by the Rust loader in the wording of this crate where the Python loader raises the error classes of its own runtime for the same failures.

Obligation PORT-5 covers the binary. The Rust binary carries the pack writing path of the Python command line and no other path in this slice. Handed the write seed pack flag and a path it writes the current pack to that path and prints the line the Python command line prints, naming the current format and the path, and it answers every other argument shape with a usage line and a nonzero exit. The field run and its gates and its reports stay with the Python command line whole.

Obligation PORT-6 states the equivalence method and the boundary of this slice. Equivalence is proven against recorded Python answers and never against a restatement. A probe program kept in the round scratch runs the Python package over the pack documents its own tests build and records what the loader answers and what the writer writes, and the committed Rust conformance tests carry those recordings and compare the Rust answers against them at content level, the entries field by field in order and the refusals word by word with the recorded path standing in for the path of each test and the written bytes byte by byte. The claimed set is every pack document that parses as JSON, at every one of the ten format versions and at every refusal the loader promises, together with the pack document surface and the writing surface. Everything else in the Python stays Python only after this slice, and it is named here so the boundary is a decision. The seed apply functions and the hand written parser for the Python source shapes they read stay Python only and form the largest remaining piece of the port. The engine that plants the seeds and runs the gates and scores the field stays Python only whole. The report module stays Python only. The command line beyond the pack writing path of PORT-5 stays Python only. On behavior any of that machinery decides, this slice claims nothing, and its conformance set holds no such case. Each later port slice retires the Python module it ports together with its pytest files, so the cargo tests become the primary suite module by module, and the share of the tree standing in each language at the end of this slice is recorded with the slice status together with this justification for the remainder, that what stays Python is the spec this slice was judged against and the machinery no slice has ported yet.

At the end of this slice the Rust stands at 1519 lines across the crate sources and the conformance test file, with the recorded conformance cases excluded as data, and the Python stands at 9183 lines across the package and its test suite, so the tree is not yet majority Rust and this section does not claim otherwise. The justification for the non Rust remainder stands as PORT-6 states it, that what stays Python is the spec this slice was judged against and the machinery no slice has ported yet, and each later slice retires the Python module it ports together with its pytest files so the cargo tests become the primary suite module by module.
## Rust port slice 2 obligations

The ruling of 2026-11-02 makes every repo in the program a Rust library, and this section states the obligations of the second port slice of canaryfield. The landed Python is the behavioral spec for everything ported here. One correction to the first slice's framing stands at the head of this section because everything in it follows from that correction. There is no hand written parser in the Python. The seed apply functions never see source text at all. They receive a CPython syntax tree built by ast.parse, mutate it, and the engine renders the mutated tree back with ast.unparse. What this slice must reproduce is therefore three CPython behaviors taken as a black box, the tree ast.parse builds, the equality ast.dump gives, and the spelling ast.unparse gives. The obligations in this section were probed against the landed Python before they were written, and the load bearing probe findings are these. A source of 5,000 nested parentheses raises a syntax error with the text "too many nested parentheses (<unknown>, line 1)". A valid small module followed by one null byte raises a syntax error with the text "source code string cannot contain null bytes". The source "def broken(:" raises a syntax error with the text "invalid syntax (<unknown>, line 1)". seed-17 over a table whose key and value are the same name returns True from its apply while the dump of the tree after the call equals the dump of the freshly parsed source, so the bool and the planting are different answers and the dump test in plant_seed, not the bool, decides what counts as planted. Duplicate application is not uniform across the family, seed-05 applying twice over the same tree and rendering the literal back on the second call while seed-01 returns False on its second call. plant_seed over the demo project plants seventeen seeds, each into sample.py alone: seed-00, seed-01, seed-02, seed-03, seed-04, seed-05, seed-06, seed-07, seed-08, seed-09, seed-10, seed-11, seed-12, seed-16, seed-20, seed-21, and seed-28. The other thirty one seeds plant nothing there. seed-04 plants because the demo module's handler body in safe_div is a raise statement, not one bare pass, and its apply replaces that body with a silent pass.

Obligation PORT2-1 covers the readings this slice gives the crate. The crate gains one apply entry per seed of the registry, taking a parsed module tree and returning whether the call planted anything, reproducing seed.apply of canaryfield/seeds.py for all forty eight seeds in registry order, and it gains plant_seed over a directory, reproducing the planted file list and the written bytes of engine.plant_seed in canaryfield/engine.py. The registry data of PORT-2 does not change. The crate's seed entries gain their apply behavior and nothing else about them moves.

Obligation PORT2-2 covers the tree. The crate reads Python source text into a tree reproducing the tree CPython 3.12 ast.parse builds for the same text over the whole grammar of that version, the TryStar and Match kinds included as standing kinds, with constants arriving as the parser folds them, a negative number standing as a unary operation over a constant and never as a negative constant, and every positioned node carrying its line number and its column offset as a UTF-8 byte offset. The tree model keeps the distinction the defaults readings split on, where a missing keyword only default is Python None itself and an explicit default of None is a constant holding None, and the two never read as one shape. A source the grammar refuses builds no tree and meets the refusal family of PORT2-7. The reading bound for nesting stands at 4,000 on the standing line rule, and the interpreter's own shallower limits, probed at 5,000 nested parentheses refusing as a syntax error, stand inside that bound as grammar refusals of the same family.

Obligation PORT2-3 covers the two renderings of a tree. The crate serialises a tree for the change test reproducing ast.dump without attributes, positions standing outside the serialisation so a change of positions alone is no change, and it spells a tree back to text reproducing ast.unparse byte for byte over every shape the recorded corpus holds. Every planting decision in this slice reads these two renderings the way the Python reads them, and no decision reads a source spelling where the Python reads a value or a tree rendering.

Obligation PORT2-4 covers collection order and the shared readings. The flat collection reproduces the ordering helper of the Python, source position order with the node type name breaking a tie at one position. The parent carrying collectors reproduce the deepest first replacement discipline of the await, yield, and negation collectors, and the loop body collectors reproduce the inside a loop body reading with its boundary rules, turning on for a loop body and off for its else clause and never crossing a function, class, or lambda boundary. The boolean refusing integer and zero predicates reproduce the Python readings exactly, booleans standing as neither integer constants nor zero literals.

Obligation PORT2-5 covers the forty eight apply functions. Each plants at every match in the tree it is given, matches collected from the tree as parsed before any change is made, each matched node planted exactly once, and a change a planting makes never creating a new match for itself inside the same call. The per seed match shapes and changes are the ones the landed functions state, including the first operator only reading of the comparison seeds, the shared arithmetic swap table of seed-06 and seed-24 under which division is never swapped, the per statement list judgement of seed-42 under which a list holding only declarations keeps them, and the test and match guard exclusion of seed-47 under which a negation standing in a test or in a match guard never plants while the same negation standing outside plants. An apply result is the bool the Python returns, including where a second application of the same seed over the same tree returns True again, and the recordings of PORT2-8 carry that answer per seed rather than an assumed idempotence.

Obligation PORT2-6 covers the scope readings. The bound names reading reproduces the Python reading whole, parameters first in signature order and then the names the body binds in source position order, over the binding forms the Python counts, never crossing a nested function or class body and never descending into a lambda or any comprehension kind. The sibling attribute reading reproduces its per scope records, with a nested definition's header read in the enclosing scope's record and its body read in a new record of its own. The own returns reading reproduces its boundary at nested scopes, descending through a function's own statements and stopping at any nested function, class, or lambda.

Obligation PORT2-7 covers plant_seed whole and the refusal family. plant_seed reproduces the candidate walk, every Python file under the root recursively in relative path string order with the ignored parts and the test file exclusions the Python applies, the symlink and resolution safety rules, the strict UTF-8 reading, and the skip list behavior under which a candidate that cannot be read as UTF-8 or cannot be parsed is skipped for every seed and named once in the skipped list when one is handed over. Where the Python lets a syntax error stand as that skip, the port's parse refusal is its own wording as a returned refusal inside the library's tree reading, and plant_seed's skip behavior over it is identical. On a change under the dump test the port fixes missing locations as the Python does, renders the tree, and checks the rendering the way the Python compile check does, and a rendering the language refuses is never written and the file does not count as planted and does not join the skipped list. A planted file is written as the rendering plus one trailing newline, and a file with no planting is never rewritten, and no guard stands around the write itself, so an error from the write propagates as the Python's does. The recursion family stands as a named port limit in this obligation. The recursive collectors and the dump and unparse renderings carry a depth ceiling the flat walk does not, and past that ceiling the landed Python answers with an uncaught recursion error, and the port refuses that family in its own wording rather than reproducing a crash, a crash standing as no reading under the standing rule of this program.

Obligation PORT2-8 covers how this slice proves itself. Proof is recorded conformance. A generator program runs the landed Python over the case corpus and records each case's inputs and the answers the Python gave, and the Rust tests read the recordings offline and compare exactly. Three answer kinds are recorded per case, the apply answers as the bool and the dump of the mutated tree, the rendered text of the mutated tree byte for byte, and the plant_seed answers as the planted file list and the final bytes of every candidate file, planted or standing. The corpus is drawn from the inputs the suite's 394 pinning tests exercise, from the demo project, and from a generated synthetic grammar corpus beyond the suite's inputs covering the families the groundwork names: second application outcomes per seed, TryStar shapes, AsyncFor shapes, a non UTF-8 candidate file, f-string and class docstring positions for seed-21, negative constants, already planted forms, guard negations, declaration only lists, and one position collection ties. No recording carries an answer written by hand, and no live differential harness stands in the gate, because the gates run with no Python in the loop.

Obligation PORT2-9 covers the retirement and the boundary. At the close of this slice canaryfield/seeds.py retires whole together with the Python tests that pin the apply surface, and plant_seed's Python body in canaryfield/engine.py retires with it into a thin standing statement that the planting stands in the crate, and the engine's remaining machinery, the copies, the git mode, the gate running, and the scoring, stands untouched until its own slice, and the recordings of PORT2-8 carry the specification from that moment. The report module, the command line beyond the pack writing path, and the field running machinery stay Python only and are named here so the boundary is a decision. The closed round count does not move in this slice's build commits. It moves at the round's closure in the owner's commit with the README line, on the standing closure pattern.

Obligation PORT2-10 covers the shape of the repository at the end of this slice and the count of its lines, counted by the method PORT-6 uses, lines of code with blank lines and comment lines standing outside the count and the recordings counted as data and standing outside both sides of the count. The closing entry states the crate and Rust test lines against the Python lines that remain after the retirement of PORT2-9, each figure counted at the final head, and states the justification the remainder needs, that the engine's gate machinery, the report module, and the command line are the named work of the slices that follow.

At the end of this slice the Rust stands at 13327 lines across the crate sources and the Rust test files, with the recorded conformance cases and the two Unicode data tables the parser carries excluded as data, and the Python stands at 720 lines across the package and its test suite, so the tree is majority Rust at 94.9 percent of the counted lines. The generator that made the recordings stands at 468 Python lines in tools and is counted on neither side, as the maker of the data the battery reads. The justification for the non Rust remainder stands as PORT2-10 states it, that the engine's gate machinery, the report module, and the command line are the named work of the slices that follow, and the setup tests that pin the standing facts of the repository remain standing as the Python suite's surviving test file beside the demo fixture module.

Amendment to PORT2-9, added by the Round 15 repair after the closing entry above was written. PORT2-9 retires canaryfield/seeds.py whole and leaves the engine machinery standing untouched, and that text stands unchanged. As built, the retirement also left the package front door, the engine, and the seed pack module importing the registry from the retired module, so the Python package could not be imported at all. The repair stands the registry on data instead. A new module, canaryfield/registry.py, reads the records at import from the seed pack document the package ships beside it, canaryfield/seed-pack.json in the canaryfield-seeds/10 format, and each record carries exactly the four fields the pack carries. A record carries no planting behaviour, and its apply refuses in words naming the Rust crate, in the same shape as the standing statement plant_seed already carries. Correction to the closing entry of PORT2-10. The Python figure in that entry was counted before this repair. At the repair head the Python stands at 837 lines across the package and its test suite, counted by the same method, and the Rust stands unchanged at 13327 lines, so the tree is majority Rust at 94.1 percent of the counted lines. A further correction to the same entry, on a recount at the final repair head by the same method. The Python figure in the correction above was counted before the repair finished. ## Rust port slice 3 obligations

The ruling of 2026-11-02 makes every repo in the program a Rust library, and this section states the obligations of the third port slice of canaryfield. The landed Python is the behavioral spec for everything this slice touches, and this slice ports no Python module and retires none. It works inside the crate, on machinery two earlier slices built, and its subject is a duplication those slices left standing. The crate carries the printable reading, which codepoints the Python repr leaves unescaped, in two places, and the string spelling built on that reading in two places. One copy stands in src/json.rs and serves the seed pack surface, its py_str_repr escaping through a private is_printable over a static table of 712 merged non printable ranges. The other stands in src/pydata.rs and src/pyunparse.rs and serves the tree surface, its is_printable reading src/printable_ranges.txt and its str_repr spelling a text constant from its codepoints. The obligations in this section were probed against the landed tree before they were written, and the load bearing probe findings are these. The two printable tables are the same set, computed over all 1,114,112 codepoints the two readings differ at 0 codepoints, and each differs from the interpreter's own isprintable reading at 0 codepoints. A seam set of 22 single codepoints across the control, format, separator, private use, and unassigned categories, with quote mixes, the backslash, and the escape whitespace shapes beside them, spells identically through both copies and through the interpreter on every recorded line. The one difference between the copies is in kind and not in any answer, and it is the lone surrogate. The tree copy takes its input as codepoints and carries U+D800, spelling it as the interpreter spells it, and the pack copy takes its input as a Rust text string, which can hold no lone surrogate at all, and the pack decoder already refuses a surrogate escape no pair completes, under PORT-4 and outside the recorded conformance. Neither table can be regenerated from anything in the tree, the pack table's generator standing in a round's scratch that no longer stands and the tree table's module header crediting a script that does not write it. At the head this section is written on, the tree stands at 14,240 Rust lines and 1,181 Python lines by the method PORT-6 uses, with tools at 629 lines counted on neither side.

Obligation PORT3-1 covers the unification itself. The static table NON_PRINTABLE_RANGES leaves src/json.rs whole, all 712 merged ranges of it standing at lines 621 to 1334 of the file at the head this section is written on, and the private is_printable that read it leaves with it, and py_str_repr leaves with them whole, the function itself and not only the escape loop inside it. What stands in src/json.rs is py_repr with its container and scalar arms unaltered, the None arm, the True and False arms, the integer text, and float_text, which is the JSON module's own machinery and no part of either repr copy. The string arm of py_repr spells its text through pyunparse::str_repr over the text's codepoints, and the object arm spells its keys the same way, the spelling the tree surface already uses, and src/seedpack.rs keeps calling json::py_repr at the same three sites, lines 214 and 222 and 233, with the same signature, so the loader's refusal texts are produced by the same entry over the unified reading.

Obligation PORT3-2 covers the surviving reading as the crate's one printable authority. After this slice every surface that spells a string reads the one table, src/printable_ranges.txt with its 711 printable ranges, through pydata::is_printable, and the codepoint test is_printable_cp in src/pyunparse.rs delegates to it as it does today and answers false for a codepoint that stands as no char. The surfaces are the pack loader's refusals through py_repr, the dump spellings of src/pydump.rs at lines 167 and 198, and the unparse spellings of src/pyunparse.rs at lines 1067 and 1113 with the str_literal_helper fallback at line 1435, and no second table or second reading stands anywhere in the crate. The lone surrogate is stated plainly so the record holds the whole truth of the seam. The pack copy's text input could present no lone surrogate, and the decoder refusal of PORT-4 already stands between any pack document and that codepoint, so no answer the pack surface gives can change when its spelling moves to the codepoint reading, and on the tree surface the unified reading spells U+D800 exactly as it does today and as the interpreter does. The unification removes the weaker input form, and it changes no answer any surface can present.

Obligation PORT3-3 covers refusal continuity on the pack surface. Every refusal the loader of PORT-4 raises keeps its standing text word for word, including the two refusals that carry a repr, the missing fields refusal carrying the repr of the entry and the field refusal carrying the repr of the whole entry, now spelled through the unified reading of PORT3-1. The recorded conformance of PORT-6 is the proof and it is not re-recorded, and the battery of PORT3-6 replays those refusals word for word against the standing recordings.

Obligation PORT3-4 covers the surviving table's generator and its provenance. A generator program stands under tools/ on the pattern of tools/gen_xid_tables.py and writes src/printable_ranges.txt from the unicodedata of the interpreter it runs under, asserting that interpreter before it writes, CPython 3.12 over Unicode 15.0.0 at the head this section is written on. Run against the tree this slice leaves, the generator's output is the standing table byte for byte, and that agreement is part of this slice's proof, because a table no generator in the tree can reproduce is the defect this slice exists to close. The header of src/pydata.rs is corrected in the same slice to name this generator as the printable table's maker, where the landed header credits tools/gen_unicode_names.py, which writes src/unicode_names.txt and src/unified_ranges.txt and does not write the printable table.

Obligation PORT3-5 covers the seed-17 discrimination at apply level. The plant level proof stands as plant:dict-nochange-17 in the PORT2 battery and needs nothing. At apply level no standing case isolates the shape, of the 910 apply cases 869 carry a seed-17 answer and 24 record a first application bool of True, and in all 24 the recorded dump differs from the case's standing dump, so the battery as it stands cannot show the bool and the planting coming apart. This slice adds exactly one synthetic corpus case to the generator of tools/gen_port2_recordings.py, over a source holding a self-mapping dictionary entry, the plant case's own d = {key: key} serving, and the case is recorded by that generator run against a tree of the head 3524fcc757311db01a623d0545cb50cfc3b12dfd, the last head before the retirement of the Python seed module under PORT2-5 and the head the standing battery was itself recorded at, whose package still carries canaryfield/seeds.py with the forty eight apply functions standing in it. At the head this section is written on the generator cannot run, because its import names canaryfield.seeds, which stands nowhere in the package at that head, and the seed objects of canaryfield/registry.py refuse application, their apply raising NotImplementedError on the stated ground that the seed applies stand in the Rust crate under PORT2-5, so no case is generated at the head this section is written on in this round. The case enters the battery only through a proof run first, a full run of the generator against that recording tree with the new case not yet added to it reproducing the standing 927 cases exactly in the generator's own output form, and only then is the case added and the run repeated, the repeated run standing as an addition against the standing battery file with no standing case altered. If the reproduction fails, the case does not enter on any other basis, and the build stops and reports the failure instead of recording the case. The new case records seed-17's first application as the bool True standing alone, with no dump key and no render key beside it, and its second application likewise, on the battery's standing convention for an application that leaves the dump and the render standing, and the replay of tests/port2_conformance.rs reads the missing keys as the standing answers, so no test code changes beyond the census assertion PORT3-6 adds.

Obligation PORT3-6 covers how this slice proves itself. Both batteries replay at the slice head against their standing recordings and every standing answer stands identical. The PORT1 battery replays its 56 load cases with the refusal texts compared word for word and its census assertion standing at 56, and the PORT2 battery replays at 928 cases, the 927 standing cases and the one addition of PORT3-5. The PORT2 replay carries no case census assertion at the head this section is written on, its count assertions standing as floors over the compared rows, so this slice adds one on the pattern of the PORT1 census, asserting 928 over the cases the replay compares, and that added assertion is new test code this slice carries and the only test code that moves. The generator run that produces the 928 shows additions only against the standing battery file. A seam pin set stands over the unified path so the one reading stands pinned in one place, spelling the seam shapes through both surfaces, the separator and format codepoints through the pack surface and the tree surface, and the lone surrogate through the tree surface, each pin carrying the interpreter's spelling as its expected answer. The slice's red proof is a source census pin in the Rust tests that reads src/json.rs as text and asserts the static table and the private is_printable stand nowhere in it, and that pin fails on the head this section is written on, where both stand, for exactly the reason it exists, and passes only when the deletion of PORT3-1 has landed.

Obligation PORT3-7 covers the boundary of this slice. The report module stays Python only whole, its port and its pins together the work of the slice that follows, as the header of tests/test_seedpack.py assigns them and as the boundary of PORT2-9 already names, and the groundwork's census of its format and its crash family, an empty rows list raising TypeError at the width computation and a missing gates or summary or clean exit or cell key raising KeyError, stands as that slice's evidence of record, and this slice claims nothing on any report behavior. The engine, the command line beyond the pack writing path, the field running machinery, the registry, and every other module in either language stand untouched, and no Python module retires in this slice, so the justification for the Python remainder stands as PORT2-10 states it. Obligation PORT3-8 covers the shape of the repository at the end of this slice and the count of its lines, counted by the method PORT-6 uses, lines of code with blank lines and comment lines standing outside the count and the recordings counted as data and standing outside both sides of the count, and with one rule stated here that the standing entries use without spelling out and the groundwork's calibration brought to light, that a line whose first characters are #[, carrying nothing but an attribute, also stands outside the count. Amendment to PORT3-5, added at the Round 16 repair after the reviews of the round's pull request. PORT3-5 states that the case enters the battery only through a proof run first, a full run of the generator against that recording tree with the new case not yet added to it reproducing the standing 927 cases exactly in the generator's own output form, and that text stands unchanged. A seed's planting answers the planted file names in the planting's own order and the unparseable names it met, and the run gathers the unparseable names across the seeds into the result's sorted list, each name standing once. A gate cell stands not-applicable where the seed planted nowhere, and gate-broken where the clean exit for that gate is not zero, and otherwise the gate runs in the planted copy and the cell stands caught where its exit is not zero and waved-through where it is zero, and the planted exits record only the gates that ran.
